              Universality is relative to the declared grammar, strata, bridges, preservation profiles, and normalization targets.




Relative universality means universality only after the grammar, strata, bridges, preservation profiles, normalization targets,
                                          and descent doctrine have been declared.
                                                       Abstract
    Komorebi Math is formulated as a relative classifying theory of resolved representations. A resolved syntax
records typed operations, packet idempotents, refinements, residuals, completions, and certificates. Block 01
constructed the common additive-Karoubi foundation and the preservation-profile gate for theorem transport.
Block 02 developed profiled strata, representation groupoids, typed directional bridges, information-loss records,
bridge-law certificates, and separate holonomy, cokernel, Brauer, and higher-coherence obstruction theories.
Block 03 proved the first heterogeneous effective-descent theorem on unit purely periodic sequences across
minimal recurrence, reduced rational ordinary generating function, minimal digit automaton, and rational
functional-Mahler mask charts.
    Block 04 removes the periodicity hypothesis on the digital and functional-Mahler overlap. For a unit series
𝐹 ∈ 1 + 𝑧F 𝑝 [[𝑧]], Frobenius gives the exact identity

                                                       𝐹 (𝑧)
                                            𝑅𝐹 (𝑧) =            = 𝐹 (𝑧) 1− 𝑝 .
                                                       𝐹 (𝑧 𝑝 )

Hence the canonical mask is rational exactly when 𝐹 𝑝−1 is rational, and every object in the selected semantic
category is algebraic over F 𝑝 (𝑧) of degree at most 𝑝 − 1. A divisor-residue class modulo 𝑝 − 1 is proved to be
the exact obstruction to rational collapse. Its vanishing characterizes the rational, hence ultimately periodic,
locus. Its nonvanishing produces automatic but non-C-finite sequences for odd 𝑝.
     For a reduced mask 𝐴/𝐵, denominator stabilization yields finite Cartier transitions on a polynomial space of
explicit dimension. The intertwining map 𝑃 ↦→ (𝑃/𝐵)𝐹 is injective. Consequently, the reachable numerator
states are exactly the 𝑝-kernel states and their linear span is exactly the canonical Cartier module. Reachability
restriction alone therefore recovers the minimal deterministic digital chart; no additional behavioral quotient
remains. Reduced rational masks, minimal reachable-observable Cartier modules, and minimal least-significant-
digit-first 𝑝-DFAOs form three equivalent normalized charts. Their fibers are contractible, so the triple-overlap
coherence law holds and the descent category is equivalent to the discrete category of unit rational-mask series.
A compatible invariant package containing the reduced mask, collapse obstruction, degree profile, Cartier
rank, 𝑝-kernel, and minimal automaton descends and is complete on this declared domain. An exact verifier
records 11,391 checks across 125 reduced masks over F2 , F3 , and F5 , with no failures. No claim is made that
every automatic sequence has a rational canonical mask or that functional Mahler equations are unrestrictedly
equivalent to Newton-Mahler interpolation.
     Block 05 removes the rational-mask hypothesis from the fixed-radix digital and linear layer. For every
field-valued sequence, the span of its radix sections is the canonical pointed digit module. A sequence is regular
exactly when this module is finite dimensional. Reachable restriction followed by observable quotient reduces
every finite digit-linear realization to this canonical module, so the minimal state-space dimension is the regular
rank. A strict shortlex kernel basis gives normalized coordinates, and a word-Hankel theorem identifies the
same number with the rank of the continuation matrix. The semantic, section-module, minimal linear, shortlex,
and word-Hankel charts form an effective finite atlas with contractible normalized fibers.
     Over a finite field, finite-dimensional span is equivalent to finite kernel orbit. The orbit is the unique
minimal DFAO, and its state count lies between the regular rank and the cardinality of the ambient vector space.
Exact bounded enumeration exhibits strict state-to-rank compression. Over characteristic zero, polynomial and
digit-sum sequences show that finite section rank can coexist with an infinite orbit. The Block 05 verifier records
89,055 exact checks. Block 06 then constructs the integral saturation and prime-power reduction layer. Its
verifier records 697,958 additional checks, bringing the Block 03–06 regression total to 838,209 checks with no
failures. Semiring minimality, cross-radix comparison, and finite-data recognition remain separate obligations.
     Block 06 passes from fields to integral and prime-power coefficient systems. For every integer-valued
sequence with finite rational section rank, the actual radix sections generate a finite free lattice 𝑀𝑘 (𝑎), while
the integer-valued part of the rational section space is a canonical finite free saturation 𝐿 𝑘 (𝑎). The quotient
𝐶 𝑘 (𝑎) = 𝐿 𝑘 (𝑎)/𝑀𝑘 (𝑎) is finite. Its Smith invariants and Fitting ideals control every modular reduction through
a digit-equivariant five-term Tor sequence. At modulus 𝑝 𝑟 , the semantic section module has explicit cardinality
and generator number, and its orbit is the unique minimal prime-power output automaton. Compatible towers
reconstruct the embedded 𝑝-adic raw lattice. Finite ghost coordinates disappear in the inverse limit, whereas
the missing saturated quotient survives as the 𝑝-primary defect. A raw orbit-closure theorem and a digit-sum
example distinguish 𝑝-adic effectivity, profinite effectivity, ordinary integral lifting, and actual section realization.
The Block 06 verifier records exact Smith, reduction, tower, orbit, and named-family checks; the result remains
confined to integer-valued sequences already known to have finite rational section rank.

Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                               Komorebi P.


          Block 07 develops the next native-invariant layer. Every integral unit series has a unique fixed-basis Euler
      exponent sequence and a unique divisor-ghost sequence; formal logarithmic differentiation gives the exact
      coefficient recurrence and Möbius inversion gives the exact integrality locus. Every integral multiplicative
      arithmetic function is effective descent from its prime-power local series, whose local Euler exponents and local
      ghosts linearize Dirichlet convolution. On the restricted locus of multiplicative Euler exponent functions, the
      divisor–ghost transform is reversible, prime-power seeds are complete, and the exponent, ordinary Euler-product,
      multiplicative-ghost, and prime-power charts form an effective four-chart atlas. The block preserves the global
      boundary between additive Cauchy indexing and multiplicative Dirichlet indexing. Its verifier records 595,964
      exact checks, bringing the Block 03–07 cumulative total to 1,434,173 checks with no failures.
          Block 08 develops the local Newton–Mahler and affine polyphase stratum. For each prime 𝑝, the classical
      Mahler transform identifies 𝐶 (Z 𝑝 , Z 𝑝 ) isometrically with coefficient sequences 𝑐𝑟 → 0. Complete clopen
      residue packets at depth 𝑝 𝑎 are reversible and satisfy a strict nested-address law. Conjugating this packet
      decomposition by the Mahler transform yields exact affine coefficient kernels
                                                                                    𝑘
                                        Θ 𝑀,𝑠 (𝑟, 𝑘) = [𝑧𝑟 ] (1 + 𝑧) 𝑠 (1 + 𝑧) 𝑀 − 1 ,

     with finite support, mixed-radix composition, and exact Chinese-remainder coherence. On finite Mahler degree
     the same integer matrix has four different bridge passports: it is invertible over characteristic zero, injective but
     generally nonsplit over Z, rank-deficient at primes dividing 𝑀, and collectively split after adjoining a sharp
     minimal packet of neighboring shifts. The local valuation ledger transports every declared monotone precision
     profile, while principal-unit state dynamics produce Mahler coefficients with linearly improving 𝑝-adic slope
     under repeated 𝑝-power refinement. The block explicitly separates this Newton–Mahler interpolation channel
     from functional-Mahler coefficient equations. Its verifier records 229,743 exact checks, bringing the Block
     03–08 cumulative total to 1,663,916 checks with no failures.

Keywords: resolved representation, packet calculus, stratified universality, Newton–Mahler coordinates, affine polyphase,
                    p-adic refinement, mixed-radix coherence, valuation profile, invariant descent.


  Central claim. Komorebi Math is a stratified classifying theory of resolved representations. The principal
  domains do not share one elementary formula or one globally canonical coordinate system. They share a
  typed resolved syntax, explicitly typed bridges, and a descent problem. The correct target is therefore not
  unrestricted universality but universality relative to declared data.


  Claim boundary. The local theorem sources, the concordance, and the assembly arguments are manuscript-
  level mathematical work. Direct proofs, imported classical theorems, exact finite computation, conjectures,
  and independent external review remain separately labeled throughout. This paper does not assert that every
  object has a finite Komorebi presentation, that all strata are equivalent, that every bridge is reversible, or that
  the full heterogeneous atlas is already a stack.




                                                              ii
Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                       Komorebi P.



    Progressive expansion ledger. Block 01 completes the common finite foundation: typed resolved
    signatures, exact packet objects, mixed transfer blocks, refinement and residual calculus, preservation-profile
    gates, proof-support certificates, and the additive-Karoubi envelope. Block 02 develops the atlas and
    obstruction interface: profiled stratum passports, normalized charts, representation groupoids, the directed
    transformation category, bridge passports, information-loss profiles, bridge-law certificates, reversible cores,
    higher coherence defects, and four typed obstruction theories. Block 03 freezes the first heterogeneous atlas
    across linear, digital, and functional-Mahler strata and proves its bridge laws, higher coherence, object and
    morphism effectivity, and complete invariant descent on the unit-periodic domain. Block 04 widens the
    digital and functional-Mahler overlap to every unit series with rational canonical mask, proves the exact
    reachable Cartier core, classifies rational collapse, and establishes a second effective heterogeneous atlas.
    Block 05 removes the rational-mask hypothesis from the fixed-radix regular layer, proves canonical section
    and word-Hankel normalization, and identifies the automatic finite-orbit locus. Block 06 constructs the
    integral raw and saturated section lattices, computes the Smith–Fitting reduction defect, builds the compatible
    prime-power tower, and separates local completion from integral and orbit effectivity. Block 07 proves
    canonical fixed-basis Euler and ghost coordinates, prime-power descent for multiplicative functions, and
    the restricted divisor–ghost product–multiplicative atlas. Block 08 proves the continuous Newton–Mahler
    coordinate atlas, complete clopen and coefficient packets, affine polyphase transport, mixed-radix coherence,
    sharp finite-degree packet recovery, valuation ladders, and principal-unit precision amplification. Later
    blocks will treat cross-radix compatibility, semiring realizations, holonomic and algebraic normal forms,
    stronger local valuation signatures, and the remaining native invariants. No uncompleted broader stratum is
    silently promoted to theorem status.

1 Introduction

1.1 From one formalism to a stratified classifying theory

The foundational papers already contain a typed quiver and module nucleus, seed presentations and seed rank, exact
packet coordinates, transfer-block algebras, refinement laws, nonlinear packet composition, operator-algebraic
completion, finite holonomy, relative obstruction objects, Galois descent, and several exact sequence and arithmetic
realizations [1, 2, 3, 6, 7]. The source audit therefore changes the central research question.
The missing step is not another reconstruction of the linear or packet foundations. It is an assembly theorem that
says when the existing local theories form one coherent object. In compressed form,

                                    local resolved theories + typed bridges
                               +coherence and effectivity + compatible invariants
                                            = stratified universality

The equality is schematic, but its terms now have precise mathematical roles. Local theories are categories of
profile-preserving models. Reversible bridges are equivalences on overlap categories. Directional transformations
are functors with explicit information-loss profiles. Coherence is the pseudofunctor law on multiple overlaps.
Effectivity means compatible local objects and morphisms actually come from a global semantic object. Invariant
completeness requires one further step: local isomorphisms supplied by local completeness must glue.


1.2 Contributions of this manuscript

This paper makes eleven main contributions.

 1. It consolidates the strongest available editions into a 55-component theorem concordance and records the
    hypotheses, imported inputs, proof class, and remaining obligation for every component.
 2. It develops the common resolved foundation in detail: typed signatures, exact packet analysis and synthesis,
    mixed transfer reconstruction, refinement, selected-support residuals, proof-support certificates, and profile-
    safe theorem transport. It then gives an explicit universal construction for the finite additive-Karoubi resolved
    envelope. This closes the linear, finite-biproduct, split-idempotent part of the envelope problem without
    pretending that nonlinear, topological, or monoidal structure is automatic.
 3. It proves the finite profiled resolved atlas assembly theorem. The theorem separates formal categorical
    assembly from the nonformal verification of the actual Komorebi bridges.
 4. It organizes four distinct global failure modes: graph holonomy, relative cokernel obstruction, Brauer
    obstruction, and higher coherence failure.
 5. It completes the first normalized cross-presentation atlas for persistent C-finite sequences. Minimal recurrence
    data, reduced rational ordinary generating functions, and minimal reachable-observable states form equivalent
    charts with effective descent. The exact torsion-Fourier packet profile is independent of the chosen chart.
 6. It proves the first genuinely heterogeneous effective-descent theorem. Unit purely periodic sequences over
    F 𝑝 glue coherently across minimal recurrence, reduced OGF, minimal digital automaton, and rational
    functional-Mahler mask charts. Every triple overlap and the quadruple coherence law is verified, objects
    and morphisms descend effectively, and a compatible invariant package is globally complete on the declared
    domain.
 7. It extends the digital-functional-Mahler overlap beyond pure periodicity. Rational canonical masks are
    characterized by the Frobenius radical identity, the exact rational-collapse obstruction is computed, the

                                                         1
Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                     Komorebi P.


    denominator-stabilized compiler is reduced to its reachable Cartier core, and mask, minimal Cartier-module,
    and minimal DFAO charts are proved to satisfy effective descent with a complete descended invariant.
 8. It proves a fixed-radix normalization theorem for the full field-valued regular stratum. The canonical section
    module, strict shortlex coordinates, minimal digit-linear realization, and finite-rank word-Hankel chart are
    equivalent. Over finite fields the orbit is the minimal automaton; over characteristic-zero fields finite rank may
    coexist with an infinite section orbit.
 9. It constructs the canonical integral saturation of a rational section module, proves the Smith–Tor and Fitting
    reduction formulas, derives compatible prime-power semantic automata, and identifies the exact obstructions
    separating 𝑝-adic, profinite, integral-lattice, and actual-section effectivity.
10. It normalizes the filtered-product and multiplicative strata: integral unit series receive unique Euler exponent
    and divisor-ghost coordinates, multiplicative functions descend from prime-power series, and a complete
    divisor–ghost atlas links multiplicative exponent data to ordinary Euler products without collapsing Cauchy
    and Dirichlet convolution.
11. It constructs the local Newton–Mahler atlas: continuous functions, canonical Mahler coefficients, complete
    clopen branch packets, and affine branch-coefficient packets form equivalent normalized charts. The finite-
    degree restriction yields sharp integral and modular packet sizes, while Carmichael–Mahler state constructors
    carry certified precision slopes into the local chart.


1.3 What is imported and what is new

The packet and transfer identities, preservation-profile theorem transport, corner representation and recognition,
relative obstruction theory, graph holonomy, Galois descent, and the sequence-specific transfer theorems are
imported from the strongest internal manuscripts [1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7, 8]. The finite additive-Karoubi envelope
theorem, the explicit finite atlas assembly statement in the form used here, the normalized C-finite three-chart
category, the heterogeneous unit-periodic atlas, and the rational-mask digital descent theorem are proved in this
manuscript. The denominator-stabilized Cartier identity and its ambient degree bound are imported from the
strongest custom-function report. The Frobenius-radical characterization, rational-collapse obstruction, exact
reachable-core theorem, and rational-mask effectivity are established here from that spine. Block 05 imports the
finite digit-section grammar from the sequence manuscript and proves the canonical reachable-observable reduction,
strict shortlex normalization, word-Hankel rank theorem, fixed-radix effective-descent theorem, and automatic
finite-orbit boundary in the form printed below. Block 06 uses standard structure theory for finitely generated
abelian groups, Smith normal form, Fitting ideals, Tor, and 𝑝-adic completion, but the canonical saturation theorem,
digit-equivariant reduction package, effectivity hierarchy, and orbit-closure formulation printed here are assembled
and proved in this manuscript. Block 07 imports the filtered-product existence theorem, the Euler-transform
recurrence, prime-power reconstruction, and multiplicative residue-packet law from the sequence manuscript [7].
The integral Euler-coordinate group isomorphism, prime-Euler normalized atlas, valuation logarithmic-derivative
identity in the printed form, multiplicativity reflection of the divisor–ghost map, and the effective restricted
product–multiplicative descent theorem are proved and assembled here. Block 08 imports the classical Mahler
coordinate theorem [11], the affine Mahler kernel and state constructors from the sequence manuscript [7], and
the sharp finite packet theorems from Core-Transfer Addendum 04 [5]. The complete clopen descent atlas, strict
mixed-radix coefficient coherence, typed four-level bridge ladder, valuation-profile transport, and exact separation
of the functional and Newton–Mahler channels are assembled and proved here under the printed hypotheses.

2 Resolved theories, packet coordinates, and preservation profiles

This section expands the common formal core used by every later atlas construction. The purpose is not to force
every domain into one category. It is to separate four layers that are often conflated:

        typed syntax −→ resolved coordinates −→ profile-preserving realization −→ domain semantics.

The first two layers are algebraic. The third records which algebraic or universal constructions survive a
realization. The fourth supplies domain-specific hypotheses such as completeness, positivity, convergence,
integrality, measurability, or analytic continuation. A cross-domain statement is a theorem only when all four layers
are present.


2.1 Typed resolved signatures

Definition 2.1 (Finite resolved signature). Fix a commutative coefficient ring k. A finite resolved signature is a
tuple
                                       K𝑚 𝑓 𝑖𝑛 = (𝑄, 𝐼, P, Ref, Comp, Cert)
with the following data.
 (i) 𝑄 = (𝑄 0 , 𝑄 1 , 𝑠, 𝑡) is a finite quiver. Its vertices are sorts and its arrows are typed primitive operations.
(ii) 𝐼 ⊳ k𝑄 is a finitely generated two-sided ideal generated by k-linear combinations of paths with common source
     and common target.
(iii) P is a finite list of packet declarations. A declaration on a sort 𝑋 consists of endomorphism words 𝑝 𝑋,𝑟 ,𝑖 and
      the relations                                                           ∑︁
                                         𝑝 𝑋,𝑟 ,𝑖 𝑝 𝑋,𝑟 , 𝑗 = 𝛿𝑖 𝑗 𝑝 𝑋,𝑟 ,𝑖 ,    𝑝 𝑋,𝑟 ,𝑖 = 1𝑋 .
                                                                         𝑖

(iv) Ref is a finite list of refinement declarations. For 𝑠 ⪰ 𝑟, an address map

                                                      𝜌 𝑠𝑟 : 𝐼 𝑋,𝑠 ↠ 𝐼 𝑋,𝑟

                                                            3
Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                       Komorebi P.


     is accompanied by the equations                               ∑︁
                                                  𝑝 𝑋,𝑟 ,𝑖 =                 𝑝 𝑋,𝑠, 𝑗 .
                                                               𝜌𝑠𝑟 ( 𝑗 )=𝑖

(v) Comp is a list of optional completion tags. A tag names a doctrine to be added later, such as Banach
    completion, a 𝐶 ∗ -involution, a multiplier completion, or a monoidal structure. A tag is not itself an existence
    theorem.
(vi) Cert is a list of named residual and certificate types. Each certificate type specifies its source object, target
     object, and the equations or inequalities it is intended to record.

The finite signature is the presentation-level object. It records legal words and declared equations. It does not
identify a preferred realization, a preferred coordinate basis, or a preferred semantic domain.

Definition 2.2 (Realization of a finite resolved signature). Let C be a k-linear category. A realization of Kfin in C
assigns an object 𝑀𝑋 to every vertex 𝑋 ∈ 𝑄 0 and a morphism

                                               𝑀𝑎 : 𝑀𝑠 (𝑎) −→ 𝑀𝑡 (𝑎)

to every arrow 𝑎 ∈ 𝑄 1 , such that all relations in 𝐼, all packet equations, and all declared refinement equations hold.
Completion and certificate tags are interpreted only when the realization profile explicitly includes them.

Theorem 2.3 (Typed realization and module equivalence, imported). Let

                                                     ΛK := k𝑄/𝐼.

In k-modules, the category of typed realizations of the linear grammar (𝑄, 𝐼) is equivalent to the category of unital
left ΛK -modules. Under the equivalence, the vertex idempotent 𝑒 𝑋 acts as the projector onto the component of sort
𝑋, and each arrow acts in its declared source-target block.

Proof. A typed realization defines the action of a path by composition and then extends k-linearly. Every element
of 𝐼 acts by zero, so the action factors through ΛK . Conversely, for a unital module 𝑀, the pairwise orthogonal
vertex idempotents give                                Ê
                                                 𝑀=         𝑒 𝑋 𝑀,
                                                         𝑋∈𝑄0

and an arrow 𝑎 : 𝑋 → 𝑌 maps 𝑒 𝑋 𝑀 into 𝑒𝑌 𝑀. These constructions are inverse up to their canonical natural
isomorphisms. This is the typed-module theorem of Paper I [1].                                          □

Remark 2.4 (Presentation is not classification). The theorem fixes the semantics of one declared grammar. It does
not say that the grammar is unique, minimal, or recoverable from a finite list of observations. Distinct quivers with
relations can present equivalent module categories, and one realization can annihilate additional syntax through the
kernel of its action.

Example 2.5 (Two-sort transfer grammar). Take two sorts 𝑋, 𝑌 , one arrow 𝑎 : 𝑋 → 𝑌 , and no nontrivial relation.
A realization is a single typed map
                                                           𝑀𝑎
                                                     𝑀𝑋 −−−→ 𝑀𝑌 .
After collapsing to 𝑀𝑋 ⊕ 𝑀𝑌 , the vertex idempotents are coordinate packets and 𝑀𝑎 is one off-diagonal transfer
block. The smallest typed grammar therefore already contains the distinction between object packets and transfer
channels.

packets are not orthogonal for the standard inner product. This separates the algebraic reconstruction theorem from
later metric statements about energy or best approximation.


2.3 Refinement, selected support, and named residuals

Definition 2.15 (Finite refinement). A packet system P 𝑠 = ( 𝑝 𝑠𝑗 ) 𝑗 ∈ 𝐼𝑠 refines P 𝑟 = ( 𝑝 𝑟𝑖 )𝑖 ∈ 𝐼𝑟 when there is a

                                               𝜌 𝑠𝑟 : 𝐼𝑠 ↠ 𝐼𝑟
such that                                                        ∑︁
                                                    𝑝 𝑟𝑖 =                 𝑝 𝑠𝑗 .
                                                             𝜌𝑠𝑟 ( 𝑗 )=𝑖

The map 𝜌 𝑠𝑟 records the address of each fine packet inside its coarse ancestor.

Proposition 2.16 (Aggregation and transitivity). A finite aggregation of pairwise annihilating packets is again a
packet system. If 𝑡 ⪰ 𝑠 ⪰ 𝑟, then refinement is transitive with address map

                                                     𝜌𝑡𝑟 = 𝜌 𝑠𝑟 ◦ 𝜌𝑡 𝑠 .

Proof. For an aggregated packet, idempotence follows because all cross terms vanish. Distinct aggregates annihilate
each other, and the total sum remains the identity. Substituting the 𝑡-level expansion into the 𝑠-level expansion
gives the composite address law.                                                                                 □
                                                               Í                       Í
Theorem 2.17 (Exact refinement law, imported). Let 𝑝 𝑟𝑖 = 𝜌𝑠𝑟 ( 𝑗 )=𝑖 𝑝 𝑠𝑗 and 𝑞 𝑢𝑎 = 𝜎𝑣𝑢 (𝑏)=𝑎 𝑞 𝑏𝑣 . For every
morphism 𝑓 : 𝑋 → 𝑌 ,
                                                     ∑︁     ∑︁
                                       𝑞 𝑢𝑎 𝑓 𝑝 𝑟𝑖 =              𝑞 𝑏𝑣 𝑓 𝑝 𝑠𝑗 .
                                                      𝜎𝑣𝑢 (𝑏)=𝑎 𝜌𝑠𝑟 ( 𝑗 )=𝑖

Coarsening the fine block product of two composable maps gives the coarse block product.

Proof. Insert the two refinement identities on the left and right of 𝑓 . For composition, expand both maps at the
fine level, multiply, and sum over descendant intermediate packets. The fine intermediate projectors sum to the
coarse intermediate projector. This is the exact refinement law of Resolution Theory [2].                       □

Definition 2.18 (Support compression and residual). For packet systems P on 𝑋 and Q on 𝑌 , let 𝐸 ⊆ 𝐼 Q × 𝐼 P .
Define                                  ∑︁

                                          (𝑎,𝑖) ∈𝐸

The first term is the retained transfer support and the second is its named residual.

Proposition 2.19 (Boolean algebra of block selectors). As endomorphisms of the k-module Hom(𝑋, 𝑌 ), the
selectors satisfy
                            𝐶𝐸2 = 𝐶𝐸 ,    𝐶𝐸 𝐶𝐹 = 𝐶𝐸∩𝐹 ,      𝑅 𝐸 = 𝐼 − 𝐶𝐸 .
In particular,
                                 𝐶𝐸 𝑅 𝐸 = 𝑅 𝐸 𝐶𝐸 = 0,             𝑓 = 𝐶𝐸 ( 𝑓 ) + 𝑅 𝐸 ( 𝑓 ).

Proof. Applying 𝐶𝐸 to a block 𝑞 𝑏 𝑓 𝑝 𝑗 retains it exactly when (𝑏, 𝑗) ∈ 𝐸. Pairwise annihilation removes every
mismatched source or target index. The formulas follow block by block.                                        □

Definition 2.20 (Relational support product). For 𝐸 ⊆ 𝐼𝑌 × 𝐼 𝑋 and 𝐹 ⊆ 𝐼 𝑍 × 𝐼𝑌 , define

                      𝐹 ◦ 𝐸 := {(𝑐, 𝑖) : there exists 𝑎 ∈ 𝐼𝑌 with (𝑐, 𝑎) ∈ 𝐹 and (𝑎, 𝑖) ∈ 𝐸 }.

Proposition 2.21 (Support propagation under composition). If 𝑓 = 𝐶𝐸 ( 𝑓 ) and 𝑔 = 𝐶𝐹 (𝑔), then

                                                     𝑔 𝑓 = 𝐶𝐹◦𝐸 (𝑔 𝑓 ).

Thus exact zero patterns propagate by relational composition.
                                   Í
Proof. Every block of 𝑔 𝑓 is a sum 𝑎 𝑔𝑐𝑎 𝑓 𝑎𝑖 . A nonzero summand can occur only along an intermediate address
𝑎 belonging to the relational product.                                                                      □

Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                      Komorebi P.


Proposition 2.22 (Exact residual product identity). Let

                          𝑓 𝐸 = 𝐶𝐸 ( 𝑓 ),     𝑟 𝑓 = 𝑅 𝐸 ( 𝑓 ),        𝑔𝐹 = 𝐶𝐹 (𝑔),      𝑟 𝑔 = 𝑅𝐹 (𝑔).

Then
                                            𝑔 𝑓 − 𝑔𝐹 𝑓𝐸 = 𝑟 𝑔 𝑓𝐸 + 𝑔𝐹 𝑟 𝑓 + 𝑟 𝑔 𝑟 𝑓 .
If the morphisms lie in a normed operator algebra with a submultiplicative norm, then

                              ∥𝑔 𝑓 − 𝑔𝐹 𝑓 𝐸 ∥ ≤ ∥𝑟 𝑔 ∥ ∥ 𝑓 𝐸 ∥ + ∥𝑔𝐹 ∥ ∥𝑟 𝑓 ∥ + ∥𝑟 𝑔 ∥ ∥𝑟 𝑓 ∥.

Proof. Substitute 𝑔 = 𝑔𝐹 + 𝑟 𝑔 and 𝑓 = 𝑓 𝐸 + 𝑟 𝑓 and expand. The norm estimate is immediate from the triangle
inequality and submultiplicativity.                                                                        □

The identity is a basic certificate-propagation law. It explains exactly which residual terms enter after composing
two compressed transfers. No asymptotic claim is required.
Theorem 2.23 (Hilbert–Schmidt optimality, imported). Assume 𝑋, 𝑌 are Hilbert spaces, the packet idempotents
are orthogonal projectors, and 𝑓 : 𝑋 → 𝑌 is Hilbert–Schmidt. Then 𝐶𝐸 is the Hilbert–Schmidt orthogonal
projection onto the selected block subspace and
                                                           ∑︁
                                        ∥𝑅 𝐸 ( 𝑓 ) ∥ 2HS =    ∥𝑞 𝑎 𝑓 𝑝 𝑖 ∥ 2HS .
                                                                 (𝑎,𝑖)∉𝐸

Consequently, 𝐶𝐸 ( 𝑓 ) is the unique best Hilbert–Schmidt approximation to 𝑓 among operators supported on 𝐸.
Boundary 2.24 (Algebraic exactness is not metric optimality). For arbitrary idempotent packets, reconstruction
and residual decomposition remain exact. Orthogonal energy identities and best-approximation statements require
a Hilbert structure, orthogonal projectors, and the stated operator class. Those hypotheses are part of the theorem
rather than consequences of the packet notation.


2.4 Preservation profiles and proof-support certificates

A realization can preserve one part of the resolved calculus and forget another. The preservation profile is therefore
a mathematical interface, not informal metadata.
Definition 2.25 (Constructor universe). Fix a declared universe ℭ of constructors and universal properties. Typical
entries are
                           0, 1, finite sums, composition, scalar action, biproducts,
                                  kernels, cokernels, quotients, tensor products,
                       packet idempotents, transfer blocks, refinements, named residuals,
                         finite limits, finite colimits, topological completions, adjoints.
The list may be enlarged, but every new entry must specify the exact identity or universal property to be preserved.
Definition 2.26 (Verified preservation profile). A verified preservation profile Π𝐹 for a functor 𝐹 : C → D
consists of a subset of ℭ together with a proof or declared comparison isomorphism showing that 𝐹 preserves each
listed constructor. We write 𝑐 ∈ Π𝐹 only after the corresponding preservation claim has been verified.
Definition 2.27 (Proof-support certificate). A proof-support certificate for a finite proof P is a finite subset

                                                       csupp(P) ⊆ ℭ

containing every constructor and universal property used in the proof. The certificate need not be minimal. It must
be sufficient and auditable.

                                                                  8
 Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                      Komorebi P.


 Proposition 2.28 (Common profile and composition). Let 𝐹 : C → D and 𝐺 : D → E. Every constructor
 preserved by both functors, with compatible chosen comparison data, is preserved by 𝐺𝐹. For a finite diagram of
 realizations and bridges, the intersection               Ù
                                               Πcommon :=    Π𝐹
                                                                𝐹

 is therefore a valid common profile for every proof transported through the whole diagram.

 Proof. Preservation equations and comparison isomorphisms compose. Universal properties remain universal
 after applying the second functor whenever both preservation witnesses are present. Intersecting the verified lists
 retains only constructors supported at every stage.                                                              □

 Theorem 2.29 (Equational realization principle, imported). Let 𝐹 : C → D be additive between preadditive
 categories. Every equality of morphism expressions built from declared generators by finite sums, additive inverses,
 identities, zero maps, and composition is preserved by 𝐹. Scalar multiplication is preserved when the categories
 are enriched over the same coefficient ring and 𝐹 is linear. If a proof additionally invokes a chosen biproduct,
 kernel, cokernel, quotient, tensor product, completion, or another universal construction, the proof transports only
 when that constructor belongs to Π𝐹 .

 Proof. Functoriality preserves identities and composition. Additivity preserves zero, sums, and additive inverses.
 Linearity preserves the declared scalar action. Structural induction on the morphism expression transports every
 equational step. When a step is characterized by a universal property, the verified profile witness identifies the
 image construction with the corresponding target construction. This is the equational realization principle of Paper
 I [1].                                                                                                            □

 Proposition 2.30 (Profile-safe theorem transport). Let P prove a statement 𝑇 in the resolved syntax, and let 𝐹 be a
 realization or bridge. Suppose
                                                 csupp(P) ⊆ Π𝐹 .
After every additional domain hypothesis used by 𝑇 has been verified in the target, 𝐹 (𝑇) is a valid target theorem.
If the inclusion fails, the source proof does not by itself establish the target statement.

 Proof. Apply theorem 2.29 to every equational and universal-construction step in the proof certificate. The
 remaining assumptions are precisely the domain-specific hypotheses, which are checked separately.         □

 Definition 2.31 (Semantic realization certificate). A semantic realization certificate for transporting a theorem
 consists of four fields:
(C1) an explicit functor or intertwining construction;
(C2) its verified preservation profile;
(C3) a proof-support certificate for the source theorem together with the inclusion into the profile;
(C4) verification of every target-specific hypothesis and a restatement of the conclusion in native target objects.

 Boundary 2.32 (Terminology is not semantic transport). Calling a frequency band, residue class, ramification
 sector, or latent channel a “packet” is only terminological reuse until the packet idempotents, reconstruction law,
 transfer blocks, and required preservation maps are supplied. Failure of a profile gate is an explicit open obligation,
 not permission to infer the desired theorem by analogy.

2.5 The finite additive-Karoubi resolved envelope

The full profile-indexed envelope remains a multi-doctrine problem. The finite linear, additive, and split-idempotent
core can be constructed exactly.
Definition 2.33 (Linear grammar category). For a finite linear resolved presentation Klin = (𝑄, 𝐼, P, Ref), let

                                                    AK := k𝑄/𝐼.

This is the k-linear category whose objects are the vertices of 𝑄 and whose morphisms are k-linear combinations
of typed paths modulo the declared relations.
Definition 2.34 (Finite additive hull). The category Add(AK ) has finite tuples

                                                 𝐴 = ( 𝐴1 , . . . , 𝐴 𝑚 )

as objects. A morphism 𝐴 → 𝐵 = (𝐵1 , . . . , 𝐵𝑛 ) is an 𝑛 × 𝑚 matrix with entries in AK ( 𝐴 𝑗 , 𝐵𝑖 ). Composition is
matrix multiplication using composition and addition in AK .
Definition 2.35 (Karoubi envelope). The Karoubi envelope Kar(Add(AK )) has pairs ( 𝐴, 𝑒), where 𝑒 : 𝐴 → 𝐴 is
idempotent, as objects. A morphism
                                            𝑓 : ( 𝐴, 𝑒) −→ (𝐵, 𝑑)
is a morphism 𝑓 : 𝐴 → 𝐵 satisfying
                                                       𝑓 = 𝑑𝑓 𝑒.
The identity on ( 𝐴, 𝑒) is 𝑒.
Theorem 2.36 (Finite additive-Karoubi resolved envelope). The category
                                                                            
                                          Synak (Klin ) := Kar Add(k𝑄/𝐼)

is small, additive, idempotent complete, and k-linear. For every additive idempotent-complete k-linear category C,
restriction to the generating quiver induces an equivalence

                                      Funk⊕ Synak (Klin ), C ≃ Modak (Klin , C),
                                                            

where the right side is the category of typed realizations satisfying all declared relations and carrying every
declared packet idempotent to a split idempotent.

Proof. There are four universal stages.
Free typed stage. The free k-linear category on 𝑄 is universal for assigning objects to vertices and morphisms to
arrows.
Relation stage. Quotienting by 𝐼 imposes exactly the declared linear, packet, and refinement equations. A functor
out of k𝑄/𝐼 is therefore the same as a typed realization satisfying those equations.
Additive stage. Every k-linear functor from k𝑄/𝐼 into an additive category extends, uniquely up to canonical
natural isomorphism, to an additive functor from the finite additive hull by sending a tuple to the corresponding
biproduct and a matrix to its induced biproduct map.
Idempotent-splitting stage. Every additive functor into an idempotent-complete category extends, again uniquely
up to canonical natural isomorphism, across the Karoubi envelope by sending ( 𝐴, 𝑒) to the chosen image of the
split idempotent 𝐹 (𝑒). Morphisms satisfying 𝑓 = 𝑑𝑓 𝑒 descend to the corresponding split summands.
Composing these universal properties yields the displayed equivalence. Smallness is preserved because only finite
tuples and idempotents in hom sets of a small category are adjoined.                                           □

                                                           11
Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                      Komorebi P.


Proposition 2.37 (Fully faithful grammar embedding). The canonical composite

                                      AK −→ Add(AK ) −→ Kar(Add(AK ))

is fully faithful. Thus the envelope adds finite biproducts and split summands without identifying two previously
distinct grammar morphisms.

Proof. The first functor sends an object to the one-term tuple and preserves its hom set exactly. The second sends
𝐴 to ( 𝐴, 1 𝐴); its morphism condition is 𝑓 = 1 𝐵 𝑓 1 𝐴, which imposes no additional relation.                  □

Corollary 2.38 (Declared packets become literal objects). Every declared idempotent 𝑝 : 𝑋 → 𝑋 defines an object
(𝑋, 𝑝) of Synak (Klin ). A complete packet family ( 𝑝 𝑖 ) gives the canonical isomorphism
                                                            Ê
                                              (𝑋, 1𝑋 )          (𝑋, 𝑝 𝑖 ).
                                                             𝑖

Corollary 2.39 (Linear resolved strata as model categories). Every additive split-idempotent realization stratum of
Klin is a category of profile-preserving functors out of Synak (Klin ).

Example 2.40 (One operator and primary packets). Take one sort, one endomorphism 𝑇, and one relation 𝜇(𝑇) = 0.
The linear grammar algebra is k[𝑥]/(𝜇). When

                                                    𝜇 = 𝜇 1 · · · 𝜇𝑟

is a product of pairwise comaximal factors, the Chinese remainder idempotents define packet objects for the primary
summands. The envelope splits those idempotents formally. Over a realization where the same factorization
remains valid, the packet objects become the corresponding invariant primary sectors.

Remark 2.41 (Universal does not mean presentation-minimal). The envelope is universal for realizations of the
declared presentation. It does not provide a canonical smallest quiver, decide equivalence of arbitrary presentations,
or identify a globally minimal packet system. Those are recognition or minimization problems inside particular
strata.


2.6 Profile-indexed extensions and the exact boundary of Block 01

Definition 2.42 (Profile-indexed resolved envelope). For a preservation profile Π, a profile-indexed resolved
envelope SynΠ (K), when it exists, is a universal source satisfying

                                        ModΠ (K, C) ≃ FunΠ (SynΠ (K), C)

for every target category C carrying the structures named by Π. The equivalence must preserve the chosen
comparison data, not only the underlying functors.

Boundary 2.43 (No single overpowered envelope). The existence of Synak does not prove the existence of one
universal category simultaneously free, complete, 𝐶 ∗ , 𝑊 ∗ , nonlinear-operadic, monoidal, and arithmetically integral
in a way preserved by every target. The mathematically safer target is a family of profile-indexed envelopes or a
doctrine-valued construction with explicit forgetful and comparison maps.

  Block 01 result. The foundational language is now explicit at four levels: typed grammar semantics, exact finite
  packet and residual calculus, proof-support-controlled theorem transport, and the universal additive-Karoubi
  envelope. The nonlinear, analytic, operator-algebraic, monoidal, and arithmetic envelopes remain separate
  profile obligations rather than hidden assumptions.

3 Strata, normalized charts, typed bridges, and obstruction objects

The common envelope developed in Section 2 is deliberately weaker than most concrete mathematical domains. The
next layer records how that envelope is realized, normalized, compared, and sometimes only partially remembered.
The central organizing rule is that a chart is not specified by a list of examples. It is specified by a category of
models, a preservation profile, a semantic functor, a normalized classification target, and explicit overlap data.


3.1 Stratum passports and normalized model categories

Definition 3.1 (Profiled stratum passport). A profiled Komorebi stratum is a record

                                          S 𝛼 = (C𝛼 , Π 𝛼 , M 𝛼 , N𝛼 , Sem 𝛼 , I𝛼 ),

with the following fields.
 (i) C𝛼 is the ambient semantic category in which the realized objects live.
(ii) Π 𝛼 is the verified preservation profile of the realization.
(iii) M 𝛼 is a full replete category of admissible models of the declared resolved syntax.
(iv) N𝛼 ⊆ M 𝛼 is a full replete normalized subcategory on which the intended inverse or classification theorem is
     stated.
(v)
                                                     Sem 𝛼 : M 𝛼 −→ O 𝛼
       is the native semantics functor.
(vi)
                                                   I𝛼 : Core(N𝛼 ) −→ D 𝛼
       is a declared invariant functor, when one has been constructed. Its codomain D 𝛼 and classification target are
       part of the record.
The original four-field stratum (C𝛼 , Π 𝛼 , M 𝛼 , Sem 𝛼 ) remains the underlying realization datum. The normalized
and invariant fields are additional assembly data rather than automatic consequences.

Definition 3.2 (Normalization witness). A normalization witness on a full subcategory U𝛼 ⊆ M 𝛼 consists of a
functor
                                             𝑁 𝛼 : U𝛼 −→ N𝛼
and a natural isomorphism
                                             𝜈 𝛼 : Sem 𝛼 𝑁 𝛼 =⇒ Sem 𝛼 | U𝛼 .
It is classification-normalizing when every object of U𝛼 is semantically isomorphic to its image, and two normalized
objects with isomorphic semantics are isomorphic in N𝛼 . A normalization may be partial, domain-restricted, or
defined only after auxiliary choices. Those restrictions belong to the passport.

Definition 3.3 (Complete invariant on a declared target). An invariant I𝛼 is complete on N𝛼 when

                                           I𝛼 (𝑋)  I𝛼 (𝑌 )       ⇐⇒      𝑋 𝑌

for all 𝑋, 𝑌 ∈ N𝛼 . This statement classifies the objects of N𝛼 up to the isomorphisms allowed there. It does not
classify arbitrary presentations outside that subcategory.



                                                             14
Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                                                 Komorebi P.


Proposition 3.4 (Semantic conservativity of a normalized chart). If 𝑁 𝛼 is classification-normalizing, then

                                                                Sem 𝛼 | N𝛼

reflects isomorphisms. Consequently, a complete invariant on N𝛼 can be transported through a reversible overlap
only after the overlap is shown to preserve the normalization and reflect the relevant isomorphisms.

Proof. Let 𝑋, 𝑌 ∈ N𝛼 and suppose their semantic objects are isomorphic. The second clause of theorem 3.2 gives
𝑋  𝑌 . The final statement follows because completeness is an assertion about isomorphism classes, not merely
about equality of numerical outputs.                                                                        □


               A chart is a typed passport, not merely a collection of examples

Boundary 3.5 (Multiple valid placements). A semantic object may occur in several strata. Catalan numbers, for
example, may be algebraic, hypergeometric, P-recursive, and tree-generated. The atlas records these as distinct
presentations with certified bridges. It does not force one placement to erase the others.

3.2 Representation groupoids, gauge levels, and the directed transformation category

For every stratum, the invertible part is the maximal subgroupoid
                                             ℜ𝔢𝔭 𝛼 (K) = Core(M 𝛼 ).
This core records changes of presentation that preserve all structure required by the overlap. Quotients, truncations,
completions, specializations, differential annihilator maps, and compression maps generally remain outside it.
Definition 3.6 (Finite coordinate action groupoid). Fix a dimension vector d = (𝑑 𝑋 ) 𝑋∈ S . Let
                                                     Ö
                                              𝐺d =       𝐺 𝐿 𝑑𝑋 (k)
                                                         𝑋∈ S
act on the finite-dimensional representation space Repd (K) by base change. The action groupoid
                                                   [Repd (K)/𝐺 d ]
has representations as objects and coordinate changes carrying one representation to another as morphisms.
Proposition 3.7 (Coordinate groupoid realizes isomorphism). Two objects of Repd (K) are isomorphic as typed
realizations exactly when they lie in the same 𝐺 d -orbit. The stabilizer of a point is its coordinate automorphism
group.

Proof. A typed isomorphism is a family of invertible linear maps on the sort spaces intertwining every generating
operation. This is precisely an element of 𝐺 d carrying one coordinate tuple to the other. Elements fixing the tuple
are exactly the typed automorphisms.                                                                              □

Three symmetry levels must remain separate.

                              Table 4: Three noninterchangeable symmetry notions.

Symmetry level          Acting object                      What it changes or preserves       Extra hypothesis
Gauge symmetry          coordinate-change group 𝐺 d or     changes presentation coordinates   an explicitly typed action
                        a local gauge family               while preserving the represented   on the chart
                                                           object
Intrinsic symmetry      Aut M 𝛼 (𝑀)                        automorphisms of one resolved      none beyond the native
                                                           object inside the stratum          category
Reconstructed           Aut ⊗ (𝜔)                          tensor automorphisms of a fiber    faithful exact tensor fiber
symmetry                                                   functor and the corresponding      functor
                                                           affine group scheme

Definition 3.8 (Typed directed transformation category). The directed transformation category 𝔇𝔦𝔯(K) has pairs
(𝛼, 𝑀), with 𝑀 ∈ M 𝛼 , as objects. A morphism
                                                 (𝛼, 𝑀) −→ (𝛽, 𝑁)
is an admitted bridge instance equipped with a bridge passport as in theorem 3.10. Composition is defined only
when the codomain profile of the first bridge satisfies the domain requirements of the second. The union of the
representation groupoids embeds as the wide invertible subcategory.
Boundary 3.9 (Invertibility is evidence, not typography). Drawing a two-headed arrow does not make a bridge
reversible. A bridge enters the representation groupoid only after an inverse, quasi-inverse, or recognition theorem
has been proved on a named overlap category.

 Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                                Komorebi P.


 3.3 Bridge passports, bridge classes, and information-loss profiles

 Definition 3.10 (Bridge passport). A certified bridge from stratum 𝛼 to stratum 𝛽 is a record
                                                                   pres
                                        𝔟 = (U𝔟 , V𝔟 , 𝐵𝔟 , 𝜏𝔟 , Π𝔟 , Π𝔟refl , Λ𝔟 , 𝜈𝔟 , 𝜒𝔟 ),

 where:
  (i) U𝔟 ⊆ M 𝛼 and V𝔟 ⊆ M 𝛽 are the declared domain and codomain subcategories;
  (ii) 𝐵𝔟 : U𝔟 → V𝔟 is the functor;
 (iii) 𝜏𝔟 is its bridge class;
         pres
 (iv) Π𝔟        records preserved constructors and universal properties;
  (v) Π𝔟refl records properties or isomorphisms reflected by the bridge;
 (vi) Λ𝔟 is the information-loss profile;
(vii) 𝜈𝔟 is the semantic comparison map;
(viii) 𝜒𝔟 records normalization, residual, and certificate compatibility.
 A passport may state that no inverse or no certificate transport is known. Missing data remain missing rather than
 being inferred from analogy.

 The permitted bridge classes follow the work program in the controlling handoff.

                                     Table 5: Bridge classes used by the concrete atlas.

 Class                           Required evidence            Interpretation

                                 coherent unit/counit

                                 and full image
                                 description
 Conservative forgetful          reflected isomorphisms       object equivalence remains detectable although structure may be
 functor                                                      forgotten
 Information-losing              explicit semantic and        quotient, projection, annihilator, reduction, or compression

                                 generate a target object

                                 completion property          theorem

 No certified bridge             none                         no arrow is inserted into the trusted atlas


 Definition 3.11 (Information-loss profile). For a functor 𝐵 : U → V, its information-loss profile contains at least:

                                 MorKer 𝐵 (𝑋, 𝑌 ) = {( 𝑓 , 𝑔) ∈ Hom U (𝑋, 𝑌 ) 2 : 𝐵 𝑓 = 𝐵𝑔},
                                             Coll 𝐵 = {(𝑋, 𝑌 ) ∈ Ob(U) 2 : 𝐵𝑋  𝐵𝑌 },
                                        EssIm(𝐵) ⊆ V,

 together with the declared constructors, packet labels, residuals, certificates, branch data, topology, or arithmetic
 structure not preserved by 𝐵. The first component records morphism collapse, the second object collapse, and the
 third which target objects actually arise.

                                                                 18
Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                        Komorebi P.


Definition 3.12 (Faithfulness, conservativity, and reversibility). A bridge is:
 (i) morphism-faithful when every MorKer 𝐵 (𝑋, 𝑌 ) is diagonal;
(ii) conservative when 𝐵 𝑓 invertible implies that 𝑓 is invertible;
(iii) classification-faithful or essentially injective on objects when 𝐵𝑋  𝐵𝑌 implies 𝑋  𝑌 on the declared
      domain;
(iv) lossless on its image when it is fully faithful;
 (v) reversible when it is an equivalence onto the declared overlap.
These conditions answer different questions and should not be conflated.

Proposition 3.13 (Composition law for bridge loss). Let
                                                         𝐵        𝐶
                                                   U−
                                                    → V −→ W

be certified bridges.
 (i) Every constructor or universal property preserved by both 𝐵 and 𝐶 is preserved by 𝐶 𝐵.
(ii)
                                          MorKer 𝐵 (𝑋, 𝑌 ) ⊆ MorKer𝐶 𝐵 (𝑋, 𝑌 ).

(iii)
                                                        Coll 𝐵 ⊆ Coll𝐶 𝐵 .

(iv)
                                                 EssIm(𝐶 𝐵) ⊆ EssIm(𝐶).

 (v) If both bridges are conservative, then their composite is conservative. If both are classification-faithful, then
     their composite is classification-faithful. If both are equivalences, then their composite is an equivalence.
Thus information forgotten at an earlier stage cannot be recovered by merely composing more forgetful bridges,
although a later bridge may attach auxiliary data through a separate constructor.

Proof. Functoriality proves the preservation claim. If 𝐵 𝑓 = 𝐵𝑔, then 𝐶 𝐵 𝑓 = 𝐶 𝐵𝑔, proving the second inclusion.
If 𝐵𝑋  𝐵𝑌 , applying 𝐶 gives 𝐶 𝐵𝑋  𝐶 𝐵𝑌 , proving the third. Every object in the essential image of 𝐶 𝐵 is visibly
in the essential image of 𝐶. For conservativity, if 𝐶 𝐵 𝑓 is invertible then 𝐵 𝑓 is invertible because 𝐶 is conservative,
and then 𝑓 is invertible because 𝐵 is conservative. For classification-faithfulness, 𝐶 𝐵𝑋  𝐶 𝐵𝑌 implies 𝐵𝑋  𝐵𝑌
and then 𝑋  𝑌 . Composition of equivalences is standard.                                                              □

Three typing cautions are especially important.

  1. A selected algebraic branch can produce a D-finite annihilator, but the operator may forget the selected branch
     and its minimal polynomial. The bridge is directional unless that data are retained.
  2. A rational functional-Mahler mask over F 𝑝 produces a Cartier representation and finite automaton under the
     stated hypotheses, but this does not identify it with a Newton–Mahler interpolation chart [8, 7].
  3. A faithful Hilbert-space corner representation realizes packet projectors and transfers, but it does not
     automatically preserve a tensor product strongly enough for Tannakian reconstruction [2, 6].

3.4 Overlap categories and bridge-law certificates

Definition 3.14 (Typed overlap). A reversible overlap between strata 𝛼 and 𝛽 consists of full replete subcategories

                                                     M 𝛼𝛽 ⊆ M 𝛼 ,               M𝛽 𝛼 ⊆ M𝛽 ,

an equivalence
                                                                               ∼
                                                           Φ 𝛼𝛽 : M 𝛼𝛽 −→ M 𝛽 𝛼 ,
and a semantic natural isomorphism

                                                    𝜎𝛼𝛽 : 𝐽 𝛼𝛽 Sem 𝛼 =⇒ Sem𝛽 Φ 𝛼𝛽

for an explicitly declared comparison functor 𝐽 𝛼𝛽 between the observable doctrines. An overlap is normalized
when the equivalence restricts to the selected normalized subcategories.
Definition 3.15 (Bridge-law certificate). A bridge-law certificate for 𝐵 : U → V records commuting or naturally
commuting diagrams for each structure used downstream:
 (i) native semantics;
(ii) packet analysis and synthesis;
(iii) refinement and coarsening maps;
(iv) selected-support compression and named residuals;
(v) proof or computation certificates;
(vi) normalization and invariant comparison maps.
For example, packet naturality requires comparison maps under which

                                               𝐵( 𝑝𝑌 ,𝑏 𝑓 𝑝 𝑋,𝑎 )  𝑝 ′𝐵(𝑌 ),𝑏 𝐵( 𝑓 ) 𝑝 ′𝐵(𝑋),𝑎 ,

                                                                          20
Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                    Komorebi P.


and refinement naturality requires the image of each coarse packet to equal the declared sum of its refined
descendants.
Proposition 3.16 (Bridge-law transport). Let P be a proof whose constructor support lies in the preserved part
of a bridge-law certificate. Then the image proof is valid in the target chart. If the proof concludes equality
of normalized invariants, that equality descends across the bridge only when the normalization and invariant
comparison maps are also included in the certificate.

Proof. The first statement is the equational realization principle of Paper I applied with the bridge-specific
preservation profile. The second statement follows because normalization and invariant functors are additional
constructors in the proof diagram; they cannot be transported when their comparison cells are absent [1].   □

On triple overlaps, pairwise equivalences are not enough. One needs comparison cells

                                            𝜂 𝛼𝛽𝛾 : Φ𝛽𝛾 Φ 𝛼𝛽 =⇒ Φ 𝛼𝛾 .

For four charts, there are two composites from Φ𝛾 𝛿 Φ𝛽𝛾 Φ 𝛼𝛽 to Φ 𝛼 𝛿 . Their ratio is the higher coherence defect.
Definition 3.17 (Quadruple-overlap coherence defect). Write horizontal composition of natural transformations as
∗. Define                            h                    i h                      i −1
                          Ω 𝛼𝛽𝛾 𝛿 = 𝜂 𝛼𝛾 𝛿 ◦ (id ∗ 𝜂 𝛼𝛽𝛾 ) ◦ 𝜂 𝛼𝛽 𝛿 ◦ (𝜂 𝛽𝛾 𝛿 ∗ id) .
It is a natural automorphism of Φ 𝛼 𝛿 on the quadruple overlap. The atlas is coherent there exactly when

                                                   Ω 𝛼𝛽𝛾 𝛿 = id.

Proposition 3.18 (Two elementary coherence mechanisms). The coherence defect vanishes in either of the following
situations.
 (i) All overlap functors compose strictly and every comparison cell is the identity.
(ii) Between each pair of relevant normalized objects there is at most one admissible comparison isomorphism
     compatible with semantics.

Proof. The first case is immediate. In the second case, the two sides of the coherence equation are admissible
comparison isomorphisms with the same source and target. Uniqueness forces them to agree. This is the mechanism
used later in the normalized C-finite three-chart atlas.                                                      □

Boundary 3.19 (Prestack versus stack). Typed overlap functors and coherent comparison cells define atlas or
prestack data. The word stack additionally requires effective descent: compatible local objects and morphisms
must arise from global ones in the declared category.


3.5 Reversible cores inside directional bridges

Directional maps can contain a canonical reversible overlap without being globally invertible. Split packet analysis
is the basic model.
Theorem 3.20 (Split bridge rectification). Let A be an additive idempotent-complete category and suppose
                                                      𝐴        𝐿
                                                   𝑋−
                                                    →𝑌 −
                                                       →𝑋

satisfies 𝐿 𝐴 = id𝑋 . Put 𝐸 = 𝐴𝐿. Then:
 (i) 𝐸 2 = 𝐸;

                                                          21
Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                      Komorebi P.


(ii) 𝑌  im 𝐸 ⊕ im(1 − 𝐸);
(iii) 𝐴 : 𝑋 → im 𝐸 is an isomorphism with inverse induced by 𝐿;
(iv) 𝐿(1 − 𝐸) = 0, so im(1 − 𝐸) is a named residual invisible to the decoder;
(v) the directional two-chart system becomes reversible after restricting the packet chart to the consistent core
    im 𝐸.
In a module or vector-space realization, im(1 − 𝐸) = ker 𝐿.

Proof. The identity 𝐸 2 = 𝐴(𝐿 𝐴)𝐿 = 𝐴𝐿 = 𝐸 proves the first statement. Idempotent completeness splits 𝐸,
producing the direct sum. The image of 𝐴 lies in im 𝐸, and 𝐿 𝐴 = id𝑋 while 𝐴𝐿 = 𝐸 acts as the identity on im 𝐸,
so the restricted maps are inverse. Finally,

                                            𝐿(1 − 𝐸) = 𝐿 − 𝐿 𝐴𝐿 = 𝐿 − 𝐿 = 0.

For modules, every 𝑦 ∈ ker 𝐿 satisfies (1 − 𝐸)𝑦 = 𝑦, while every vector in im(1 − 𝐸) is killed by 𝐿.                □

Corollary 3.21 (Exact meaning of a decoded packet core). A left inverse proves exact recovery on the consistent
packet core. It does not prove that every ambient packet vector is consistent, that the residual is small, or that the
packet representation is compressed. Those are separate geometric, analytic, or coding claims.

The integral Mahler packet theorem is a concrete instance: the analysis matrix has an integer left inverse, the
consistency projector is 𝐴𝐿, and the residual is the complementary packet component. The same decoder survives
every base change, while rank loss of individual residue channels remains visible after reduction [5].


3.6 Finite holonomy and the maximal path-flat core

Finite graph transport gives the sharpest reversible gluing model in the Library. Let Γ = (𝑉, 𝐸) be a finite connected
graph. Attach a finite-dimensional k-vector space 𝐹𝑣 to each vertex and an isomorphism 𝑇𝑒 : 𝐹𝑢 → 𝐹𝑣 to each
oriented edge 𝑒 : 𝑢 → 𝑣, with 𝑇𝑒¯ = 𝑇𝑒−1 . Choose a root 𝑜 and spanning tree 𝑆. If 𝐺 𝑣 : 𝐹𝑜 → 𝐹𝑣 is tree transport,
then every non-tree edge 𝑒 : 𝑢 → 𝑣 has fundamental holonomy

                                               𝐻𝑒 = 𝐺 𝑣−1𝑇𝑒 𝐺 𝑢 ∈ 𝐺 𝐿(𝐹𝑜 ).

Define                              Ù
                             𝐶𝑜 =         ker(𝐻𝑒 − 𝐼) = Fix⟨𝐻𝑒 : 𝑒 ∉ 𝑆⟩,          𝐶 𝑣 = 𝐺 𝑣 𝐶𝑜 .

Theorem 3.22 (Maximal flat core and global sections). Under the preceding hypotheses [3, 5]:
 (i) evaluation at the root identifies the space of global parallel sections with 𝐶𝑜 ;
(ii) (𝐶𝑣 ) 𝑣 is path-flat, meaning that all path transports with the same endpoints agree on the core;
(iii) it is the unique greatest path-flat subfamily;
(iv) it is independent of the chosen spanning tree and equals the common fixed space of all closed-path holonomies;
(v) for                                             Ê
                                     Δ𝑆 : 𝐹𝑜 −→           𝐹𝑜 ,        Δ𝑆 𝑥 = ((𝐻𝑒 − 𝐼)𝑥)𝑒∉𝑆 ,

Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                                        Komorebi P.


(vi) the full fibers glue to one global frame exactly when every fundamental holonomy is the identity;
(vii) a gauge change conjugates the holonomies and transports the core, so dim 𝐶𝑜 is gauge-invariant.

Proof. For 𝑥 ∈ 𝐶𝑜 , set 𝑥 𝑣 = 𝐺 𝑣 𝑥. Tree edges satisfy the parallel relation by construction, and a non-tree edge
satisfies it because 𝐻𝑒 𝑥 = 𝑥. Conversely, any global section is determined along the tree by its root value; every
remaining edge forces that value to be fixed by the corresponding 𝐻𝑒 . This proves the section classification. Closed
paths are words in fundamental cycles, which gives path-flatness and tree independence. Any path-flat family must
be fixed by every fundamental cycle and is therefore contained in (𝐶𝑣 ) 𝑣 . The rank formula is rank–nullity. Full
flatness and gauge covariance follow from spanning-tree normalization and conjugation of loop products.             □

Corollary 3.23 (Global section versus global frame). Nonidentity holonomy can leave a nonzero maximal core.
Therefore the existence of some global compatible vectors is weaker than the existence of one global frame on the
full fibers. The former is measured by dim 𝐶𝑜 ; the latter requires 𝐶𝑜 = 𝐹𝑜 .

3.7 Relative cokernel obstructions and loss under coarsening

Holonomy addresses compatibility of invertible transports. Correction problems have a different canonical object.

Definition 3.24 (Linear correction datum). A linear correction datum is a map

                                                            𝐷 : 𝑈 −→ 𝑉

and a defect 𝑟 ∈ 𝑉. A correction is 𝑢 ∈ 𝑈 satisfying 𝐷𝑢 = −𝑟. The relative obstruction class is

                                          Ob𝐷 (𝑟) = [𝑟] ∈ coker 𝐷 = 𝑉/im 𝐷.

Theorem 3.25 (Cokernel criterion, universality, and naturality). For a correction datum (𝐷, 𝑟) [3]:
 (i) a correction exists if and only if Ob𝐷 (𝑟) = 0;

                                                                 23
Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                    Komorebi P.


(ii) every linear obstruction test 𝜑 : 𝑉 → 𝑊 satisfying 𝜑𝐷 = 0 factors uniquely through 𝑉 → coker 𝐷;
(iii) for every commuting square
                                                      𝐷
                                              𝑈      −→    𝑉
                                              ↓𝑎           ↓𝑏        𝑏𝐷 = 𝐷 ′ 𝑎,
                                                      𝐷′
                                              𝑈′     −−→ 𝑉 ′ ,
    there is a unique induced map
                                                   𝑏 : coker 𝐷 −→ coker 𝐷 ′
    with
                                                   𝑏 Ob𝐷 (𝑟) = Ob𝐷 ′ (𝑏𝑟).

Proof. The class vanishes exactly when 𝑟 ∈ im 𝐷, which is equivalent to solvability of 𝐷𝑢 = −𝑟. A map
annihilating im 𝐷 factors uniquely through the quotient. In the square, the quotient map for 𝐷 ′ , composed with 𝑏,
vanishes on im 𝐷, so the same universal property produces 𝑏.                                                     □

Proposition 3.26 (Packet coordinates on the obstruction quotient). Suppose (𝑄 𝑎 ) is a complete finite family of
pairwise annihilating idempotents on 𝑉 and

                                                   𝑄 𝑎 (im 𝐷) ⊆ im 𝐷

for every 𝑎. Then the 𝑄 𝑎 descend to quotient idempotents 𝑄 𝑎 on coker 𝐷,

If 𝑈 and 𝑉 already split into matching packet summands and 𝐷 is block diagonal, this becomes
                                                     Ê

Off-diagonal correction blocks can couple packet defects, so the block-diagonal hypothesis cannot be omitted.

Proof. The invariance of im 𝐷 makes [𝑣] ↦→ [𝑄 𝑎 𝑣] well defined. Idempotence, annihilation, and reconstruction
descend to the quotient. In the block-diagonal case the image of 𝐷 is the direct sum of the images of the
restrictions.                                                                                                □

A coarse representation can erase a nonzero fine obstruction. The strengthened Paper III gives the exact bookkeeping
device.

Theorem 3.27 (Relative obstruction exact sequence). Suppose the diagram

                                   0 →  𝑈0 → 𝑈 𝑓                 →  𝑈𝑐 → 0
                                       ↓ 𝐷0  ↓ 𝐷𝑓                  ↓ 𝐷𝑐

commutes and has exact rows. Then there is a natural exact sequence
                                                                          𝛿
                                0 −→ ker 𝐷 0 −→ ker 𝐷 𝑓 −→ ker 𝐷 𝑐 −
                                                                   → coker 𝐷 0
                                   −→ coker 𝐷 𝑓 −→ coker 𝐷 𝑐 −→ 0.

If a fine obstruction maps to zero in coker 𝐷 𝑐 , then it comes from coker 𝐷 0 , the obstruction sector removed by
coarsening. Coarse vanishing therefore does not imply fine vanishing.

                                                           24
Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                        Komorebi P.


Proof. This is the direct kernel–cokernel sequence proved in the strengthened Paper III. The connecting map lifts
an element of ker 𝐷 𝑐 to 𝑈 𝑓 , applies 𝐷 𝑓 , identifies the result in 𝑉0 , and takes its class modulo im 𝐷 0 . Exactness
verifies independence of the lift and identifies each successive kernel with the preceding image [3, 4].              □

Example 3.28 (A fine obstruction erased by summation). Take all correction spaces to be zero,

                                     𝑉 𝑓 = k2 ,     𝑉𝑐 = k,             𝑝(𝑥, 𝑦) = 𝑥 + 𝑦.

The fine defect (1, −1) is nonzero in coker 𝐷 𝑓 = 𝑉 𝑓 , but its coarse image is zero. It lies entirely in

                                             𝑉0 = ker 𝑝 = span{(1, −1)}.

This is cancellation by coarsening, not correction of the fine defect.

Definition 3.29 (Mapping cone). For a cochain map 𝐷 : 𝐶 • → 𝐸 • , define

                           Cone(𝐷) 𝑛 = 𝐸 𝑛 ⊕ 𝐶 𝑛+1 ,        𝑑Cone (𝑒, 𝑐) = (𝑑 𝐸 𝑒 + 𝐷𝑐, −𝑑𝐶 𝑐).

Proposition 3.30 (Cohomological correction criterion). For a cocycle 𝑒 ∈ 𝐸 𝑛 , the class of (𝑒, 0) in 𝐻 𝑛 (Cone(𝐷))
vanishes exactly when [𝑒] lies in the image of 𝐻 𝑛 (𝐷). When the complexes are concentrated in degree zero,

                                              𝐻 0 (Cone(𝐷))  coker 𝐷.

Proof. The class of (𝑒, 0) is a boundary exactly when there are 𝑒 ′ ∈ 𝐸 𝑛−1 and 𝑐 ∈ 𝐶 𝑛 with

                                            𝑒 = 𝑑 𝐸 𝑒 ′ + 𝐷𝑐,            𝑑𝐶 𝑐 = 0.

This is equivalent to [𝑒] = 𝐻 𝑛 (𝐷) [𝑐]. Concentration in degree zero reduces the cone quotient to 𝐸 0 /im 𝐷.         □

Boundary 3.31 (The correction map is problem data). The cokernel is canonical relative to 𝐷. The theory does not
yet supply one choice-free rule that constructs the correct 𝐷 for every mathematical problem and every stratum.
Calling [𝑟] ∈ coker 𝐷 canonical must not hide this dependence.


3.8 Semilinear effectivity, projective descent, and the Brauer class

Galois descent gives a second exact local-to-global model. Its linear and projective cases separate effectivity from
obstruction sharply.

Theorem 3.32 (Effective vector descent by Hilbert 90). Let 𝐿/𝐾 be finite Galois with group 𝐺. Suppose

                                       𝑎 𝜎 𝜏 = 𝑎 𝜎 𝜎(𝑎 𝜏 ),            𝑎 𝜎 ∈ 𝐺 𝐿 𝑛 (𝐿),

defines a semilinear packet cocycle. Then there is 𝐵 ∈ 𝐺 𝐿 𝑛 (𝐿) such that

                                                   𝑎 𝜎 = 𝐵 −1 𝜎(𝐵).

The twisted fixed packet
                                                       𝑊 = (𝐿 𝑛 ) 𝐺,★
is an 𝑛-dimensional 𝐾-vector space and
                                                                   ∼
                                                          → 𝐿𝑛.
                                                   𝑊 ⊗𝐾 𝐿 −
Thus every finite linear semilinear atlas of this form is effective [6].



                                                              25
Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                 Komorebi P.


Theorem 3.33 (Brauer obstruction to projective packet descent). Projective semilinear descent data define a class

                                              𝛼 ∈ 𝐻 1 (𝐺, 𝑃𝐺 𝐿 𝑛 (𝐿)).

The connecting map for
                                   1 −→ 𝐿 × −→ 𝐺 𝐿 𝑛 (𝐿) −→ 𝑃𝐺 𝐿 𝑛 (𝐿) −→ 1
produces
                                          𝛿(𝛼) ∈ 𝐻 2 (𝐺, 𝐿 × ) = Br(𝐿/𝐾).
The projective atlas lifts to linear semilinear descent data exactly when 𝛿(𝛼) = 0. When the class is nonzero,
the descended object is naturally a module over a central simple 𝐾-algebra split by 𝐿, rather than an ordinary
𝐾-vector space [6].

Proof. This is the nonabelian cohomology exact sequence together with nonabelian Hilbert 90. Vanishing of the
connecting class gives a 𝐺 𝐿 𝑛 (𝐿)-valued cocycle; theorem 3.32 then trivializes it and constructs the descended
vector space. A nonzero class is the classical relative Brauer obstruction.                                    □

Corollary 3.34 (Projective compatibility is not linear effectivity). A projective atlas may satisfy every visible
overlap equation in 𝑃𝐺 𝐿 𝑛 and still have no global vector-space realization. Projective cocycle coherence and
vanishing Brauer class are distinct conditions.

The tensor reconstruction problem lies beyond ordinary corner realization.

Theorem 3.35 (Tannakian boundary). Let T be a neutral Tannakian category over 𝑘 and

                                                 𝜔 : T −→ Vect 𝑘

a faithful exact tensor fiber functor. Then
                                                  G = Aut ⊗ (𝜔)
is an affine group scheme and
                                                  T ≃ Rep 𝑘 (G)
as tensor categories. A faithful projector-corner representation without verified exact strong monoidal structure
does not cross this boundary [6, 2].

Proposition 3.36 (Typed comparison principle for obstruction objects). An obstruction object transports from one
problem to another only after a compatibility morphism for the underlying data has been supplied:
 (i) commuting squares of correction maps induce maps on cokernels;
(ii) group homomorphisms or intertwiners compatible with edge transports induce maps on holonomy data and
     fixed cores;
(iii) morphisms of the relevant central extensions induce maps on cohomological descent obstructions;
(iv) modifications of atlas pseudofunctors transport higher coherence defects.
Absent such typed data, there is no canonical reduction of one obstruction family to another.

Proof. Each item follows by applying the functorial construction defining the obstruction: quotient universality
for cokernels, conjugation or intertwining for path products, functoriality of cohomology for Brauer classes, and
naturality of horizontal and vertical composition for coherence cells. Since the input categories and codomains
differ, no comparison exists without an explicit map between the inputs.                                        □

4 The theorem concordance and edition control

4.1 Audit result

The concordance contains 55 components. Thirty-seven are proved internally, eight are classical theorems imported
and reinterpreted, two are proved conditionally, two are definition or framework rows, and six remain conjectural.
The conjectural entries are concentrated in the cross-stratum assembly layer. The local packet, obstruction, Galois,
sequence, and Mahler components are substantially more complete than an older open-question ledger suggests.


4.2 Material supersession decisions

Three corrections are mathematically consequential.


Relative obstruction theory. Older Paper III editions leave a broad universal packet-obstruction principle
conjectural. The strengthened edition replaces the finite-projector formulation by the relative class [𝑟] ∈ coker 𝐷,
with vanishing, naturality, packet decomposition, quotient projectors, relative exact sequences, and mapping-cone
formulations proved for declared correction data [3, 4]. The unresolved problem is not the quotient construction
itself. It is a choice-free mechanism assigning the appropriate correction datum to arbitrary problems and strata.



                                                              29
 Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                        Komorebi P.


 Paper I edition policy. The complete symbolic core remains the theorem-ID and proof-status spine. The
 expanded clarified edition strengthens exposition and hypotheses without replacing the stable record identifiers [1].


 Core-Transfer status. Addendum 04 replaces the isolated 𝑚 = 6, 𝑑 = 3 boundary by the exact composite-
 modulus rank law, sharp integral packet minimum, universal decoder, and all-degree stabilization [5]. The former
 example is now a sharp negative control inside a general theorem.


 4.3 Existing bridges and exact invariant slices

 The strongest source edition already contains the following exact or effective bridges on declared domains:

   • constant recurrence ↔ rational ordinary generating function ↔ finite linear state;
   • P-recursive ↔ D-finite coefficient translation;
   • finite digit realization ↔ finite 𝑘-kernel;
   • rational functional-Mahler mask → Cartier representation → finite automaton;
   • finite-mask Cartier transitions ↔ carry matrices;
   • selected algebraic branch → packet polynomial system;
   • invertible transform ↔ transported packet resolution;
   • Euler-product exponent coordinates ↔ logarithmic-derivative recurrence data;
   • finite modular state → local Newton-Mahler interpolation;
   • local Mahler coordinates ↔ affine polyphase branches;
   • finite resolved 𝐶 ∗ -system → faithful Hilbert packet realization.

 The persistent C-finite stratum has the strongest complete transfer invariant. Active spectral and torsion-phase
 data determine the exact minimal recurrence, reduced denominator, Hankel rank, state dimension, rank drops,
 and periodic packet profile of every arithmetic subsequence [7]. Restricted exact coordinate systems also exist
 for fixed-radix automata, filtered Euler products, multiplicative prime-power seeds, local Mahler charts, finite
 semisimple Galois descent, and finite seed lattices. None of these restricted results is a single all-stratum classifier.

                                                       ℜ : Ǎ op → Cat;

(A4) coherent equivalences on the reversible overlaps and separately typed directional transformations;
