11.5 Orbit closure and three levels of effectivity

Let
                                          O𝑘 (𝑎) = {Λ𝑤 𝑎 : 𝑤 ∈ Σ∗𝑘 } ⊆ 𝑀𝑘 (𝑎)
be the actual section orbit, and let
                                                            raw
                                                           O𝑘, 𝑝𝑟 (𝑎)

be its image in 𝑀𝑘 (𝑎)/𝑝 𝑟 𝑀𝑘 (𝑎).

Theorem 11.18 (Raw orbit-closure descent). The reduction maps
                                                 raw            raw
                                                O𝑘, 𝑝𝑟+1
                                                         (𝑎) ↠ O𝑘, 𝑝𝑟 (𝑎)

are surjective, and
                                                       𝑝    ∼      raw
                                              O𝑘 (𝑎)       −→ lim O𝑘, 𝑝𝑟 (𝑎),
                                                              ←−−
                                                                 𝑟

where the closure is taken in 𝑀𝑘, 𝑝 (𝑎).

Proof. A section word at level 𝑟 is lifted by the same word at level 𝑟 + 1, proving surjectivity. Every 𝑝-adic limit of
orbit points gives a compatible tower. Conversely, choose for each compatible residue state an orbit representative
𝑥𝑟 . Compatibility gives
                                                𝑥𝑟+1 − 𝑥𝑟 ∈ 𝑝 𝑟 𝑀𝑘 (𝑎),
so (𝑥𝑟 ) is Cauchy in 𝑀𝑘, 𝑝 (𝑎). Its limit lies in the orbit closure and maps to the prescribed tower.                □

Definition 11.19 (Three effectivity obstructions). Let 𝑥 ∈ 𝑀𝑘, 𝑝 (𝑎).
 1. The 𝑝-adic effectivity condition is simply 𝑥 ∈ 𝑀𝑘, 𝑝 (𝑎); every compatible full semantic module tower satisfies
    it by theorem 11.17.
 2. The integral lattice obstruction is

                                             𝜖 int (𝑥) = [𝑥] ∈ 𝑀𝑘, 𝑝 (𝑎)/𝑀𝑘 (𝑎).

      It vanishes exactly when 𝑥 is represented by one integral lattice element.
 3. The section-orbit obstruction is membership in

                                                       𝑀𝑘 (𝑎) \ O𝑘 (𝑎).

      Even after integral effectivity holds, the lift need not be an actual radix section.

Boundary 11.20 (Semantic orbit towers). The inverse limit of the full semantic modules is always 𝑀𝑘, 𝑝 (𝑎). For
the smaller semantic orbit sets, surjectivity of reduction remains automatic, but lifting to an actual orbit closure can
be obscured by finite ghost fibers. The unconditional orbit-closure theorem is therefore stated for the raw orbit
tower. A semantic-orbit effectivity theorem requires a compatible choice of raw lifts or an equivalent fiberwise
Mittag–Leffler condition.

Example 11.21 (Digit sum separates all three levels). For the base-𝑘 digit sum 𝑠 𝑘 ,

                                             O𝑘 (𝑠 𝑘 ) = {𝑠 𝑘 + 𝑐 : 𝑐 ∈ Z ≥0 },

and
                                            𝑀𝑘 (𝑠 𝑘 ) = 𝐿 𝑘 (𝑠 𝑘 ) = Z𝑠 𝑘 ⊕ Z1.

are nonnegative integers and therefore produce actual section states 𝑠 𝑘 + 𝑐 𝑁 . They converge in Z 𝑝 to

which is not an ordinary integer. Thus 𝑠 𝑘 + 𝑐 ∞ is 𝑝-adically effective and lies in the orbit closure, but its integral
obstruction is nonzero.
By contrast, 𝑠 𝑘 − 1 is an integral lattice point and lies in the 𝑝-adic orbit closure because −1 ∈ Z 𝑝 is a limit of

                                        Ö                           Ö
                              𝑀 𝑘 (𝑎)     𝑀𝑘, 𝑝 (𝑎),     𝐿 𝑘 (𝑎)    𝐿 𝑘, 𝑝 (𝑎),

                                                                                 80
Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                        Komorebi P.


and a short exact sequence
                                        0 −→ 𝑀
                                              𝑘 (𝑎) −→ 𝐿 𝑘 (𝑎) −→ 𝐶 𝑘 (𝑎) −→ 0.
                                                        

A compatible all-moduli raw tower has an ordinary integral lift exactly when its class in

                                                          𝑀 𝑘 (𝑎)/𝑀 𝑘 (𝑎)
                                                          

vanishes.

Proof. Finite free abelian groups have profinite completion equal to the product of their 𝑝-adic completions. In
Smith coordinates, the inclusion is multiplication by 𝑑𝑖 in each profinite coordinate. Its cokernel is

                                                              Z  Z/𝑑𝑖 Z.
                                                         Z/𝑑𝑖 b
                                                         b

Taking the direct sum over 𝑖 gives 𝐶 𝑘 (𝑎). The final assertion is the definition of belonging to the diagonal image of
the integral lattice inside its profinite completion.                                                                □

Corollary 11.23 (Local-global separation). Every compatible prime-power tower is effective in a 𝑝-adic completion.
A compatible family over all primes is effective in the profinite completion. Neither statement alone implies an
ordinary integral lift. Integral effectivity is the separate diagonal condition

                                                      𝑥 ∈ 𝑀𝑘 (𝑎) ⊆ 𝑀
                                                      b             𝑘 (𝑎).


This is the precise sense in which prime-power effectivity and integral effectivity differ. Completion solves the
Cauchy problem in the declared topology. It does not force the resulting point to lie in the original discrete lattice.


11.7 Canonical integral record and directional reduction atlas

Definition 11.24 (Canonical integral regular record). The canonical integral record of 𝑎 is
                                                                                       
                         I𝑘 (𝑎) = 𝑉Q (𝑎), 𝑀𝑘 (𝑎), 𝐿 𝑘 (𝑎), 𝐶 𝑘 (𝑎), (Λ𝑑 )0≤𝑑<𝑘 , 𝑎, ev0 .

Its Smith–Fitting profile is
                                                                                                      
                               SF 𝑘 (𝑎) = 𝑑1 , . . . , 𝑑 𝜌 ; Fitt0 (𝐶 𝑘 (𝑎)), . . . , Fitt𝜌−1 (𝐶 𝑘 (𝑎)) .

The record is canonical but not a single field-valued normal form. It retains both the raw generative lattice and the
maximal integer-valued lattice, together with the exact quotient between them.

Theorem 11.25 (Integral normalization and directional local reduction). For fixed 𝑘, the assignment

                                                            𝑎 ↦−→ I𝑘 (𝑎)

is functorial for digit-module morphisms preserving the distinguished sequence and evaluation. It is uniquely
determined by the semantic sequence. For every prime 𝑝, reduction defines a directional bridge
                                                                       
                                                              sem
                                     I𝑘 (𝑎) ↦−→ 𝑀 𝑘, 𝑝𝑟 (𝑎), K𝑘, 𝑝𝑟 (𝑎)
                                                                                       𝑟 ≥1

whose exact information-loss passport is:
 1. finite-depth ghost kernel 𝐶 𝑘 (𝑎) [ 𝑝 𝑟 ];
 2. finite-depth missing quotient 𝐶 𝑘 (𝑎)/𝑝 𝑟 𝐶 𝑘 (𝑎);


                                                                  81
Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                       Komorebi P.


 3. completed essential image 𝑀𝑘, 𝑝 (𝑎) ⊆ 𝐿 𝑘, 𝑝 (𝑎);
 4. integral effectivity obstruction 𝑀𝑘, 𝑝 (𝑎)/𝑀𝑘 (𝑎);
 5. actual-section obstruction inside the integral lattice.

Proof. Every component of I𝑘 (𝑎) is defined from the semantic sequence and its digit sections. A compatible
morphism carries actual sections to actual sections and integer-valued rational combinations to integer-valued
rational combinations, so it induces maps on 𝑀𝑘 (𝑎), 𝐿 𝑘 (𝑎), and the quotient. The local reduction statements are
theorems 11.7, 11.17 and 11.19.                                                                                 □

  Block 06 result. For every integer-valued fixed-radix sequence that is regular over Q, the actual sections
  generate a canonical finite free lattice 𝑀𝑘 (𝑎), and the integer-valued part of the rational section space is
  a canonical finite free saturation 𝐿 𝑘 (𝑎). Their Smith–Fitting quotient controls every modular reduction
  exactly. At each prime power, the coefficient sequence has a finite minimal semantic section automaton. The
  compatible tower reconstructs the 𝑝-adic raw lattice, while finite ghost coordinates vanish in the inverse limit
  and the missing saturated quotient survives as the 𝑝-primary defect. 𝑝-adic, profinite, integral-lattice, and
  actual-section effectivity are distinct and are separated by explicit obstruction quotients.

  Exact boundary of Block 06. The results classify saturation and reduction defects for integer-valued
  sequences that are already known to have finite rational section rank. They do not prove finite-data recognition
  of that hypothesis, a minimal realization theorem over arbitrary rings or semirings, a base-independent integral
  normal form, or compatibility across different radices. The prime-power tower is a directional local reduction
  system, not an equivalence between integral and modular presentations.



12 Filtered Euler products, prime-power seeds, and the divisor–ghost bridge

The next native-invariant layer has two different index monoids. Ordinary generating functions use the additive
monoid of coefficient addresses: Cauchy multiplication combines indices by addition. Arithmetic functions use the
multiplicative monoid of positive integers: Dirichlet convolution combines divisor addresses by multiplication.
These structures meet exactly in selected local and logarithmic-derivative coordinates, but they are not globally
interchangeable. This section constructs the fixed-basis filtered-product normal form, the prime-power normal
form for multiplicative arithmetic functions, and one rigorously typed cross-stratum bridge between them.



                                                         82
Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                        Komorebi P.


12.1 Filtered products and coefficient stabilization

Definition 12.1 (Coefficientwise filtered product). Let 𝑅 be a commutative ring. A filtered factor family is a
sequence
                                𝐹𝑚 (𝑧) ∈ 1 + 𝑧 𝑤 (𝑚) 𝑅[[𝑧]],    𝑤(𝑚) −→ ∞.
Its coefficientwise product is                          Ö
                                                              𝐹𝑚 (𝑧),
                                                        𝑚≥1

where the coefficient of 𝑧 𝑁 is computed from the finitely many factors satisfying 𝑤(𝑚) ≤ 𝑁.

Proposition 12.2 (Filtered product completion). Every filtered factor family has a unique coefficientwise product
in 1 + 𝑧𝑅[[𝑧]]. For every 𝑁, the truncation modulo 𝑧 𝑁 +1 is equal to the product of the finitely many factors with
𝑤(𝑚) ≤ 𝑁.

Proof. Fix 𝑁. Every factor with 𝑤(𝑚) > 𝑁 is congruent to 1 modulo 𝑧 𝑁 +1 , so only finitely many factors can affect
the coefficient prefix through degree 𝑁. These finite products are compatible as 𝑁 increases. Their inverse limit in
the 𝑧-adic filtration is the required series. Uniqueness follows because every coefficient is fixed at a finite stage. □

The proposition supplies existence and exact finite-prefix evaluation. It does not assert that an arbitrary factor family
is structurally compressed. A finite grammar requires an effective rule for the factors and a proof of stabilization;
otherwise the factor list can merely store the coefficient data in another form.


12.2 Canonical Euler coordinates

Let
                                                  UZ = 1 + 𝑧Z[[𝑧]]
be the multiplicative group of integral unit series, and let

                                                        EZ = ZN ≥1

be the additive group of integer exponent sequences.

Definition 12.3 (Euler-coordinate map). For 𝑏 = (𝑏 𝑚 ) 𝑚≥1 ∈ EZ , define
                                                     Ö
                                         E (𝑏) (𝑧) =     (1 − 𝑧 𝑚 ) −𝑏𝑚 .
                                                           𝑚≥1

The product is coefficientwise filtered because the 𝑚th factor is congruent to 1 modulo 𝑧 𝑚 .

Theorem 12.4 (Integral Euler-coordinate classification). The map

                                                E : (EZ , +) −→ (UZ , ·)

is a group isomorphism. Consequently, every 𝐴(𝑧) ∈ 1 + 𝑧Z[[𝑧]] has a unique representation
                                                Ö
                                       𝐴(𝑧) =         (1 − 𝑧 𝑚 ) −𝑏𝑚 ,   𝑏 𝑚 ∈ Z.
                                                𝑚≥1

Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                         Komorebi P.


Proof. Filtered multiplication gives
                                                E (𝑏 + 𝑏 ′ ) = E (𝑏)E (𝑏 ′ ).
To prove surjectivity and uniqueness, construct the exponents recursively. Suppose 𝑏 1 , . . . , 𝑏 𝑛−1 have been chosen
so that                                Ö
                                 𝐴(𝑧)      (1 − 𝑧 𝑚 ) 𝑏𝑚 ≡ 1 + 𝑟 𝑛 𝑧 𝑛 (mod 𝑧 𝑛+1 ).
                                         𝑚<𝑛
Because
                                        (1 − 𝑧 𝑛 ) −𝑟𝑛 ≡ 1 + 𝑟 𝑛 𝑧 𝑛     (mod 𝑧 𝑛+1 ),
the unique choice 𝑏 𝑛 = 𝑟 𝑛 cancels the degree-𝑛 residual. Every 𝑟 𝑛 is integral because all preceding operations
occur in Z[[𝑧]]. Induction produces one exponent sequence with the required product. If two exponent sequences
differed first at 𝑛, their quotient would have nonzero coefficient (𝑏 𝑛 − 𝑏 ′𝑛 )𝑧 𝑛 modulo 𝑧 𝑛+1 , contradicting equality.
Hence the representation is unique.                                                                                     □

Corollary 12.5 (Finite-prefix triangularity). For each 𝑁, the coefficient prefix
                                                                       ∑︁
                                    (𝑎 1 , . . . , 𝑎 𝑁 ) of 𝐴(𝑧) = 1 +    𝑎𝑛 𝑧𝑛
                                                                               𝑛≥1

and the exponent prefix (𝑏 1 , . . . , 𝑏 𝑁 ) determine one another by integral triangular recursions. No exponent 𝑏 𝑚
with 𝑚 > 𝑁 affects the coefficient prefix through degree 𝑁.

                                            Ö                       Ö 1 − 𝑧 2𝑚

                                            𝑚≥1                     𝑚≥1

Thus the distinct-part and odd-part products become the same object after normalization in the fixed Euler basis.
This is an exact equality inside the declared product grammar, not a claim that arbitrary product grammars possess
one preferred factorization.
Boundary 12.11 (Coordinate universality versus compact structural realization). Theorem 12.4 gives a unique
coordinate sequence for every integral unit series. It does not make every such series finitely describable. If the
exponent sequence is merely an arbitrary list encoding all coefficients, the representation is storage universality. A
compact seed grammar additionally requires an effective finite rule for the exponent sequence or factor family.


12.4 Multiplicative arithmetic functions as prime-power descent

For arithmetic functions 𝑓 , 𝑔 : N ≥1 → Z, write
                                                                  ∑︁

                                                                  𝑑 |𝑛

for Dirichlet convolution. Its identity is 𝜀(1) = 1 and 𝜀(𝑛) = 0 for 𝑛 > 1.
Definition 12.12 (Integral multiplicative category). Let MultZ be the discrete category whose objects are
multiplicative arithmetic functions 𝑓 : N ≥1 → Z satisfying

                              𝑓 (1) = 1,      𝑓 (𝑚𝑛) = 𝑓 (𝑚) 𝑓 (𝑛)               when (𝑚, 𝑛) = 1.

The monoidal operation is Dirichlet convolution.
Proposition 12.13 (Dirichlet inverse and multiplicative closure). If 𝑓 (1) = 1, then 𝑓 has a unique integral Dirichlet
inverse. If 𝑓 and 𝑔 are multiplicative, then 𝑓 ∗𝐷 𝑔 and 𝑓 −1𝐷 are multiplicative.

                                                                         ∑︁
                                     𝑔(1) = 1,         𝑔(𝑛) = −                 𝑓 (𝑛/𝑑)𝑔(𝑑).
                                                                         𝑑 |𝑛
                                                                         𝑑<𝑛

Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                    Komorebi P.


Hence the inverse exists integrally and uniquely. The standard coprime-divisor factorization of a divisor of 𝑚𝑛
proves closure of multiplicative functions under convolution; uniqueness of the inverse then proves multiplicativity
of 𝑓 −1𝐷 .                                                                                                        □

12.6 The divisor–ghost cross-stratum bridge

The Euler ghost transform can itself be written in the Dirichlet-convolution language. Let 1(𝑛) = 1, let id(𝑛) = 𝑛,
and let pointwise multiplication be denoted by a centered dot.

Definition 12.19 (Divisor–ghost transform). For an arithmetic exponent function 𝑏 : N ≥1 → Z, define

                                                               𝔊(𝑏) = 1 ∗𝐷 (id ·𝑏),

12.7 A restricted product–multiplicative atlas

Definition 12.22 (Multiplicative Euler-overlap category). Let EulMultZ be the discrete category of multiplicative
exponent functions
                                          𝑏 : N ≥1 → Z,       𝑏(1) = 1.
Associate to 𝑏 the four normalized charts
                                                                                                         
               B (𝑏) = 𝑏,      A (𝑏) = 𝐴𝑏 (𝑧),         C (𝑏) = 𝔊(𝑏),           P (𝑏) = 𝑏( 𝑝 𝑒 ), 𝑐( 𝑝 𝑒 ) 𝑝,𝑒 .

Theorem 12.23 (Effective descent on the divisor–ghost overlap). The exponent, fixed Euler-product, multiplicative
ghost, and prime-power seed charts form a finite normalized atlas

                                                             ℜDG .

Every bridge is reversible on the declared overlap, every multiple-overlap comparison is the unique identity over
the represented exponent function, and

                                             Desc(ℜDG ) ≃ EulMultZ .

Descent is effective for objects and morphisms.

Proof. The Euler-product chart is uniquely inverted by Theorem 12.4. The ghost chart is uniquely inverted by
Theorem 12.20. The prime-power chart is uniquely inverted by multiplicativity and unique factorization. Hence
every normalized fiber over 𝑏 is a singleton. All comparison cells and all multiple-overlap coherence laws are
therefore forced to be identities. A compatible local family glues to its common exponent function, and morphism
effectivity is automatic because the chart categories are discrete.                                            □

Theorem 12.24 (Complete descended prime-power invariant). The prime-power package

                                       JDG (𝑏) = (𝑏( 𝑝 𝑒 ), 𝑐( 𝑝 𝑒 )) 𝑝 prime, 𝑒≥0

subject to
                                      𝑝 𝑒 𝑏( 𝑝 𝑒 ) = 𝑐( 𝑝 𝑒 ) − 𝑐( 𝑝 𝑒−1 )   (𝑒 ≥ 1)
is complete on EulMultZ . It reconstructs 𝑏, 𝑐, the Euler product 𝐴𝑏 , and the coefficient recurrence of 𝐴𝑏 .

Proof. Prime-power values reconstruct the multiplicative functions 𝑏 and 𝑐. Theorem 12.4 reconstructs 𝐴𝑏 , and
Theorem 12.8 reconstructs its coefficient sequence from 𝑐. Therefore equal invariant packages imply equality in
every chart.                                                                                                  □

                                               ∑︁

                                      𝜎𝑟+1 ( 𝑝 𝑒 ) = 1 + 𝑝 𝑟+1 + · · · + 𝑝 𝑒 (𝑟+1) .
Thus one simple multiplicative exponent rule simultaneously produces a filtered ordinary product, a multiplicative
divisor-sum ghost, and a one-dimensional geometric local rule at every prime.

12.8 Why the bridge is restricted

Proposition 12.26 (The multiplicative exponent locus is not a product subgroup). The set of multiplicative Euler
exponent functions is not closed under the addition of exponent coordinates. Consequently, multiplication of the
corresponding ordinary Euler products does not define an internal group law on EulMultZ .

Proof. The constant function 𝑏(𝑛) = 1 is multiplicative. But 𝑏 + 𝑏 has value 2 at 1 and therefore is not multiplicative
under the normalization 𝑓 (1) = 1. Since ordinary Euler-product multiplication adds exponents, the multiplicative
exponent locus is not closed under that operation.                                                                    □

Proposition 12.27 (Euler coefficients are not multiplicative semantics). The coefficient sequence of 𝐴𝑏 (𝑧) need
not be a multiplicative arithmetic function even when 𝑏 is multiplicative. For example, 𝑏(𝑛) = 1 gives the partition
numbers, and
                                             𝑝(6) = 11 ≠ 𝑝(2) 𝑝(3) = 6.

Boundary 12.28 (Prime-power data require multiplicativity). Two arbitrary arithmetic functions can agree on
every prime power and differ on a composite supported at more than one prime. Prime-power seeds are complete
only after the multiplicativity axiom has been declared. A compact local seed rule also does not remove the
computational cost of factoring the input integer.

Boundary 12.29 (No universal additive–multiplicative equivalence). The divisor–ghost transform is an exact
invariant bridge on a named essential image. It is not an algebra isomorphism between ordinary Cauchy convolution
and Dirichlet convolution, does not identify the coefficients of an Euler product with a multiplicative function, and
does not produce a canonical factorization across arbitrary product grammars. Any larger bridge must name its
transform, kernel, essential image, and information-loss profile.

12.9 Exact finite verification

The Block 07 verifier performs four exact audits. First, it exhaustively checks the coefficient, exponent, and ghost
triangular maps for every exponent prefix in {−2, −1, 0, 1, 2}6 and every unit coefficient prefix in {−2, −1, 0, 1, 2}5 .
Second, it constructs deterministic prime-power systems, reconstructs global multiplicative functions, and verifies
local Euler inversion, local logarithmic derivatives, and Dirichlet convolution. Third, it checks the divisor–ghost
bridge and the power family 𝑏(𝑛) = 𝑛𝑟 . Fourth, it verifies the named local Euler coordinates printed in Table 20.
The recorded 595,964 exact checks have no failures.

  Exact boundary of Block 07. The fixed Euler basis gives a canonical coordinate system for integral unit series,
  and prime-power seed series give a canonical coordinate system for integral multiplicative functions. The
  divisor–ghost transform produces one complete cross-stratum overlap on multiplicative exponent functions.
  The block does not prove compactness for arbitrary exponent data, a minimal product compiler, closure of
  the multiplicative exponent slice under ordinary product multiplication, a global Cauchy–Dirichlet algebra
  isomorphism, or a canonical factorization across unrestricted product grammars.


  Block 07 result. Integral unit series admit unique fixed-basis Euler exponents and unique divisor ghosts, with
  exact logarithmic-derivative recurrence and finite-prefix triangularity. Integral multiplicative functions are
  effective descent from prime-power local series; local Euler exponents and local ghosts linearize Dirichlet
  convolution and form complete invariants. On the multiplicative exponent locus, the divisor–ghost transform
  gives a reversible four-chart atlas linking exponent functions, ordinary filtered Euler products, multiplicative
  ghosts, and prime-power seeds. This closes one explicit product–multiplicative invariant bridge while
  preserving the global distinction between additive and multiplicative index monoids.



13 Local Newton–Mahler coordinates, affine polyphase descent, and precision
   ladders

Block 08 develops the local stratum promised by the work program. Its purpose is not to merge every use of the
word “Mahler.” It freezes one precise local semantic doctrine, constructs its normalized coefficient and polyphase
charts, types the characteristic-zero, integral, and residue-level affine channels separately, and identifies the exact
data needed to pass from finite modular dynamics to certified local analytic packets.

Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                    Komorebi P.


13.1 Two Mahler languages and the declared local doctrine

Two constructions in the Library use the same historical name but have different variables and different semantics.

 1. A functional-Mahler equation
                                                   𝐹 (𝑧) = 𝑅(𝑧)𝐹 (𝑧 𝑝 )
    is an identity for a formal coefficient series in the variable 𝑧. Cartier operators act on the coefficient index.
    Blocks 03 and 04 use this channel over finite fields.
 2. A Newton–Mahler expansion                                         
                                                               ∑︁     𝑥
                                                     𝑓 (𝑥) =      𝑐𝑟
                                                             𝑟 ≥0
                                                                      𝑟
    is a coordinate expansion for a function of a 𝑝-adic input variable 𝑥. Affine substitutions 𝑥 = 𝑀𝑞 + 𝑠 produce
    polyphase branches of the input domain.

The present block treats the second channel. Fix a prime 𝑝. Let

                                                  C𝑝 = 𝐶 (Z 𝑝 , Z 𝑝 )

with the uniform norm, and let
                                              
                                  𝑐 0 (Z 𝑝 ) = 𝑐 = (𝑐𝑟 )𝑟 ≥0 : 𝑐𝑟 ∈ Z 𝑝 , 𝑐𝑟 −→ 0

with the coefficient sup norm.

Theorem 13.1 (Mahler coordinate theorem). The synthesis map
                                                                                 !
                                                                            ∑︁   𝑥
                              M 𝑝 : 𝑐 0 (Z 𝑝 ) −→ C𝑝 ,      𝑐 ↦−→ 𝑥 ↦−→      𝑐𝑟      ,
                                                                        𝑟 ≥0
                                                                                 𝑟

is an isometric isomorphism of Z 𝑝 -modules. Its inverse is

                                                    𝑐𝑟 = Δ𝑟 𝑓 (0).

In particular, the Newton–Mahler coefficient sequence is a canonical complete coordinate for a continuous
Z 𝑝 -valued function.

Proof. This is the classical Mahler coordinate theorem [11]. Finite Newton interpolation gives the coefficient
identity on nonnegative integers. Continuity implies that the forward-difference coefficients tend to zero, so
the binomial series converges uniformly on Z 𝑝 . Conversely, every coefficient sequence tending to zero gives a
uniformly convergent continuous function. Uniqueness follows from the finite-difference formula.             □

Definition 13.2 (Exact valuation profile and certified class). For 𝑓 = M 𝑝 (𝑐), define

                                          𝜈 𝑓 (𝑟) = 𝑣 𝑝 (𝑐𝑟 ) ∈ Z ≥0 ∪ {∞}.

If 𝑤 : N → Z ≥0 is nondecreasing and tends to infinity, put

                          M 𝑤 = {𝑐 ∈ 𝑐 0 (Z 𝑝 ) : 𝑣 𝑝 (𝑐𝑟 ) ≥ 𝑤(𝑟)},       C𝑤 = M 𝑝 (M 𝑤 ).

The function 𝑤 is a declared precision certificate. The class C𝑤 is not asserted to be a characterization of every
possible notion of local analyticity.

13.2 Complete clopen packets and effective local descent

For 𝑎 ≥ 1 and 0 ≤ 𝑠 < 𝑝 𝑎 , define the branch

                                                    B𝑎,𝑠 𝑓 (𝑞) = 𝑓 ( 𝑝 𝑎 𝑞 + 𝑠),                    𝑞 ∈ Z𝑝,

and the complete packet                                                      
                                                                B𝑎 𝑓 = B𝑎,𝑠 𝑓 0≤𝑠< 𝑝 𝑎 .

Theorem 13.3 (Complete clopen packet equivalence). The analysis map
                                                                                     𝑎 −1
                                                                                    𝑝Ö
                                                                  B𝑎 : C𝑝 −→                 C𝑝
                                                                                      𝑠=0

is an isometric isomorphism. Its inverse is the piecewise synthesis
                                                                              
                                                               𝑥−𝑠
                                               (S𝑎 𝑔) (𝑥) = 𝑔𝑠                        for 𝑥 ∈ 𝑠 + 𝑝 𝑎 Z 𝑝 .
                                                                𝑝𝑎

Thus the clopen residue cover of Z 𝑝 has effective descent for both objects and equality morphisms.

Proof. The cosets 𝑠 + 𝑝 𝑎 Z 𝑝 are pairwise disjoint, clopen, and cover Z 𝑝 . Restriction followed by the displayed
synthesis is the identity. Conversely, synthesis followed by restriction recovers every component. Continuity is
checked separately on each clopen component, and the uniform norm is the maximum of the branch norms. □

Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                                Komorebi P.


Theorem 13.4 (Strict nested-branch law). For 𝑎, 𝑏 ≥ 1, 0 ≤ 𝑠 < 𝑝 𝑎 , and 0 ≤ 𝑡 < 𝑝 𝑏 ,

                                                 ( 𝑓 𝑎,𝑠 ) 𝑏,𝑡 = 𝑓 𝑎+𝑏,𝑠+ 𝑝 𝑎 𝑡 .

All multiple-overlap comparison cells for the nested 𝑝-power branch atlas are identities. Hence its higher coherence
is strict rather than merely up to a chosen natural isomorphism.

Proof. Direct substitution gives

                           𝑓 𝑎,𝑠 ( 𝑝 𝑏 𝑞 + 𝑡) = 𝑓 ( 𝑝 𝑎 ( 𝑝 𝑏 𝑞 + 𝑡) + 𝑠) = 𝑓 ( 𝑝 𝑎+𝑏 𝑞 + 𝑠 + 𝑝 𝑎 𝑡).

The address is uniquely determined in the interval 0 ≤ 𝑠 + 𝑝 𝑎 𝑡 < 𝑝 𝑎+𝑏 .                                                   □

A single branch B𝑎,𝑠 is not injective on C𝑝 when 𝑎 ≥ 1: any nonzero continuous function supported on another
clopen coset lies in its kernel. The complete packet is therefore the reversible object. This observation will be
contrasted below with the finite-degree characteristic-zero polynomial chart, where one affine branch is invertible.

13.4 Finite-degree bridge ladder and collective recovery

The same integer kernel matrix has different categorical status after changing the coefficient doctrine. Fix 𝑑 ≥ 0
and write

                                                                       𝛽𝑝

     A local channel loses residual rank exactly at primes dividing 𝑀.
 4. A complete neighboring packet can recover the source integrally. The canonical prefix of min(𝑀, 𝑑 + 1)
    standard shifts has an integer left inverse, valid after base change to every commutative ring.
                                                                 
Proof. The diagonal follows from the leading term of 𝑀𝑞+𝑠    𝑟     , or from the generating identity for Θ 𝑀,𝑠 . The
determinant gives the integral index statement. Modulo 𝑝, the first nonzero source degree in target row 𝑘 is 𝛽 𝑝 𝑘,
which gives the rank formula. For the prefix packet, choose from channel 𝑠 and target row 𝑘 the equation indexed
by 𝑛 = 𝑀 𝑘 + 𝑠. The selected square coefficient matrix is unit lower triangular, so its inverse is integral and remains
a left inverse after every base change.                                                                              □

Theorem 13.9 (Sharp standard packet size). Define
                                                                       
                                 𝐵(𝑀) = max {1} ∪ {𝑝 𝑣 𝑝 ( 𝑀 ) : 𝑝 | 𝑀 } .

The least number of standard affine channels whose stacked map has an integer left inverse is

                                            𝐿 Z (𝑀, 𝑑) = min(𝑑 + 1, 𝐵(𝑀)).

The prefix {0, 1, . . . , 𝐿 Z (𝑀, 𝑑) − 1} attains the minimum.
For a fixed modulus 𝑁 ≥ 2, set
                                                                                 
                                 𝐵 𝑁 (𝑀) = max {1} ∪ {𝑝 𝑣 𝑝 ( 𝑀 ) : 𝑝 | gcd(𝑀, 𝑁)} .

The least packet size for a left inverse over Z/𝑁Z is

                                          𝐿 𝑁 (𝑀, 𝑑) = min(𝑑 + 1, 𝐵 𝑁 (𝑀)).

The exponents 𝑣 𝑝 (𝑁) do not change the minimum; the first residue layer already detects the obstruction.

Proof. This is the primary-rank and Smith/Hermite criterion of Core-Transfer Addendum 04. At a prime 𝑝 | 𝑀,
selected shifts are visible only through their residues modulo 𝑝 𝑣 𝑝 ( 𝑀 ) . A prefix of the stated length has full rank at
every obstructing prime, hence a unimodular-row left inverse. Any smaller packet fails at a prime attaining the
largest relevant primary component. A nonzero first-layer kernel vector lifts to a kernel vector at every higher
prime-power depth.                                                                                                       □

Theorem 13.10 (Exact branchwise valuation ledger). For every refined coefficient,
                                                                                              
                                𝑣 𝑝 (𝑑 𝑠,𝑘 ) ≥      min         𝑣 𝑝 (𝑐𝑟 ) + 𝑣 𝑝 (Θ 𝑀,𝑠 (𝑟, 𝑘)) .
                                                 𝑘 ≤𝑟 ≤ 𝑀 𝑘+𝑠


If 𝑣 𝑝 (𝑐𝑟 ) ≥ 𝑤(𝑟) for a nondecreasing weight 𝑤, then

                                                      𝑣 𝑝 (𝑑 𝑠,𝑘 ) ≥ 𝑤(𝑘).

Thus every declared monotone Mahler precision ladder survives affine polyphase refinement.

Proof. The transfer sum is finite. Apply the non-Archimedean triangle inequality. Since 𝑟 ≥ 𝑘, monotonicity gives
𝑤(𝑟) ≥ 𝑤(𝑘), and every kernel entry is integral.                                                               □

Define the min-plus certificate transform
                                                                                             
                               (𝔚 𝑀,𝑠 𝑤) (𝑘) =          min         𝑤(𝑟) + 𝑣 𝑝 (Θ 𝑀,𝑠 (𝑟, 𝑘)) .
                                                     𝑘 ≤𝑟 ≤ 𝑀 𝑘+𝑠


This transform is a certified lower bound. It need not be the exact output valuation, because cancellation can
increase the valuation beyond the termwise minimum.

13.6 Carmichael–Mahler state constructors and precision amplification

Finite modular state dynamics provide a directional constructor into the local Mahler chart. Let

                                           𝑣 𝑛+1 = 𝐶𝑣 𝑛 ,            𝑎 𝑛 = 𝑤T𝐶 𝑛 𝑣0,

with 𝐶 ∈ 𝑀𝑑 (Z 𝑝 ) invertible modulo 𝑝.

Theorem 13.12 (Matrix Carmichael–Mahler constructor). Assume

                                                 𝐶 𝐻 = 𝐼 + 𝑝 𝐿 𝐵,               𝐿 ≥ 1.



                                                                100
Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                    Komorebi P.


For every address 𝑗,
                                                                         
                                                        ∑︁
                                                                   𝐿𝑟    𝑥 T 𝑗 𝑟
                                          𝑎 𝐻 𝑥+ 𝑗 =           𝑝            𝑤 𝐶 𝐵 𝑣0,
                                                        𝑟 ≥0
                                                                         𝑟

where the series converges for 𝑥 ∈ Z 𝑝 . Its Mahler coefficients satisfy

                                                        𝑣 𝑝 (𝑐 𝑗,𝑟 ) ≥ 𝐿𝑟,

and
                                              Δ𝑟𝐻 𝑎 𝑛 ≡ 0           (mod 𝑝 𝐿𝑟 ).

Proof. Use
                                              𝐶 𝐻 𝑥+ 𝑗 = 𝐶 𝑗 (𝐼 + 𝑝 𝐿 𝐵) 𝑥
and expand the principal-unit matrix power by the convergent binomial series. One forward difference in 𝑥
contributes the factor 𝑝 𝐿 𝐵.                                                                           □

Theorem 13.13 (Principal-unit precision amplification). If 𝑋 = 𝐼 + 𝑝 𝐿 𝐵, then for every 𝑎 ≥ 0 there exists
𝐵 𝑎 ∈ 𝑀𝑑 (Z 𝑝 ) such that
                                                        𝑎
                                                 𝑋 𝑝 = 𝐼 + 𝑝 𝐿+𝑎 𝐵 𝑎 .
Consequently, for 𝐻 𝑎 = 𝐻 𝑝 𝑎 , every branch 𝑥 ↦→ 𝑎 𝐻𝑎 𝑥+ 𝑗 has Mahler coefficients satisfying

                                               𝑣 𝑝 (𝑐 𝑎, 𝑗,𝑟 ) ≥ (𝐿 + 𝑎)𝑟.

Each additional 𝑝-power refinement contributes at least one further digit of precision per Mahler depth.

Proof. Induct on 𝑎. If 𝑋𝑎 = 𝐼 + 𝑝 𝐿+𝑎 𝐵 𝑎 , the binomial expansion of 𝑋𝑎𝑝 has linear term divisible by 𝑝 𝐿+𝑎+1 , and
every higher term has at least the same valuation. Apply theorem 13.12 at the refined jump.                        □

The state-to-local construction is directional. A local branch can forget unreachable state directions, nonminimal
coordinates, or modes annihilated by the output functional. Recovering a state presentation requires a separate
recognition or minimality theorem and is not part of the Carmichael–Mahler constructor itself.

13.7 The exact boundary with functional-Mahler masks

Proposition 13.15 (No canonical unrestricted bridge between the two Mahler channels). Functional-Mahler mask
data over F 𝑝 does not determine a unique Newton–Mahler chart over Z 𝑝 , and a continuous Newton–Mahler
function does not in general determine a rational functional-Mahler mask.

Proof. A residue sequence ( 𝑎¯ 𝑛 ) admits many integral lifts. If (𝑎 𝑛 ) is one lift, then (𝑎 𝑛 + 𝑝𝑛) is another with the
same reduction modulo 𝑝. Their Newton–Mahler coefficients differ by the nonzero coefficient sequence of 𝑝𝑛.
Thus the residue-series or functional-mask presentation cannot select one local interpolation without a declared
lifting rule.
Conversely, a continuous function 𝑓 : Z 𝑝 → Z 𝑝 canonically determines its Newton–Mahler coefficients, but
rationality of an ordinary generating series or of the mask 𝐹 (𝑧)/𝐹 (𝑧 𝑝 ) is an additional property of a separate
coefficient-series presentation. Continuity alone does not supply it.                                           □


  Exact local boundary. Block 08 proves a complete normalized atlas for continuous Z 𝑝 -valued functions
  and their complete 𝑝 𝑎 -branch packets. It proves exact affine coefficient transport, mixed-radix coherence,
  finite-degree characteristic-zero invertibility, integral split recovery, modular rank loss, valuation preservation,
  and principal-unit precision amplification under the printed hypotheses. It does not identify every continuous
  function as locally analytic, compute exact higher valuations of every Θ 𝑝 𝑎 ,𝑠 (𝑟, 𝑘), choose an optimal
  mixed-radix refinement tree, or construct the combined spectral–local invariant requested by the broader
  program.

13.8 Exact examples and finite verification

For 𝑓 (𝑥) = 𝑥 2 , the source Mahler vector is
                                                               (𝑐 0 , 𝑐 1 , 𝑐 2 ) = (0, 1, 2).
The two dyadic branches are                                                     
                                                                            𝑞     𝑞
                                                                𝑓 (2𝑞) = 4     +8 ,
                                                                            1     2
and                                                                
                                                             𝑞       𝑞
                                         𝑓 (2𝑞 + 1) = 1 + 8      +8     .
                                                             1       2
This example exhibits exact finite-window coefficient transport.
For a principal unit 𝑢 = 1 + 𝑝,                                                 
                                                                      𝑥
                                                                             ∑︁ 𝑥    𝑟
                                                                    𝑢 =      𝑝     .
                                                                        𝑟 ≥0
                                                                                𝑟
The branch 𝑥 = 𝑝𝑞 has coefficients                                              𝑘
                                                                          𝑢𝑝 − 1 ,
whose valuation is at least 2𝑘. The branch therefore improves the original slope-one certificate.
At 𝑝 = 2, 𝑀 = 2, and 𝑑 = 3, each single reduced channel has rank two, while the two-channel packet has rank four.
This is the smallest visible instance of collective recovery.
The verifier checks the affine generating identity, finite polynomial transport, triangular determinants, mixed-radix
composition, Chinese-remainder diamonds, nested branch addresses, prime-power Frobenius sparsity, exact local
ranks, packet minimality, integer decoders, composite primary minima, valuation ledgers, scalar principal-unit
amplification, and the refined Fibonacci matrix congruences. It also includes a negative control showing that equal
residue data can have distinct Newton–Mahler lifts.

  Block 08 result. The local continuous, Newton–Mahler coefficient, complete clopen branch, and branch-
  coefficient presentations form an effective normalized atlas. Affine polyphase kernels compose strictly and
  transport declared valuation ladders. The finite-degree restriction exposes a four-level bridge ladder: reversible
  characteristic-zero charts, nonsplit integral channels, rank-deficient residue channels, and split neighboring
  packets. Carmichael–Mahler transfer embeds finite principal-unit state dynamics into the local atlas with a
  certified precision slope that improves under 𝑝-power refinement. Functional-Mahler masks remain a distinct
  typed channel unless coefficient lifts and interpolation data are supplied.



14 Completion program for the heterogeneous atlas

14.1 Correct dependency order

The remaining heterogeneous work has a strict topological order:

                         profiled envelope −→ declared atlas −→ typed bridge laws
                     −→ higher coherence −→ effective descent −→ compatible invariants

The first three arrows cannot be skipped. Pairwise formulas do not themselves define a pseudofunctor. Local
completeness does not itself construct compatible global isomorphisms.

           from external                                            imported classical theorem, conjecture, and
           validation                                               independent peer review in distinct status fields.


14.4 Finite completion criterion

A rigorous first heterogeneous stratified-universality theorem should be declared complete only after the following
finite milestone is met:

 1. a profile-indexed envelope is constructed for every profile used by the selected atlas;
 2. at least two genuinely different normalized strata are fully specified as categories;
 3. every overlap bridge is typed and its information loss is stated;
 4. every nonempty triple-overlap coherence equation is proved;
 5. descent for objects and morphisms is effective;
 6. one nontrivial native invariant family has coherent comparison maps and a proved global completeness
    theorem on the normalized descent category;
 7. the theorem concordance and source-edition ledger are synchronized with the final manuscript.

The C-finite theorem completes a three-chart presentation atlas within one stratum. Theorems 8.10 and 8.12 meet
all seven items for a restricted atlas spanning three strata. Theorems 9.11 and 9.12 enlarge the digital and functional-
Mahler overlap from unit pure periodicity to every unit series with rational canonical mask. Theorems 10.6, 10.10
and 10.11 close fixed-base field-valued regular normalization. Theorems 11.3, 11.7 and 11.25 close the canonical
integral saturation and prime-power reduction package for integer-valued sequences already known to have finite
rational section rank. Theorems 12.4, 12.15, 12.23 and 12.24 now close fixed-basis product normalization,
multiplicative prime-power normalization, and one explicit cross-stratum invariant overlap. Theorems 13.1,
13.3, 13.7, 13.9 and 13.13 now close the continuous Newton–Mahler chart, complete local polyphase descent,
finite-degree collective recovery, and the principal-unit precision ladder. The remaining program is therefore
a cross-radix, semiring, holonomic, algebraic, nonlinear, stronger local-valuation, optimal mixed-radix, and
unrestricted product-grammar compatibility problem rather than a missing elementary Mahler-coordinate theorem.


15 Exact boundaries and permitted claims

15.1 Claims supported at manuscript level

The following statements are now supportable under the printed hypotheses:

  • Komorebi Math has a substantial common resolved syntax and many proved local realization theories.
  • The finite additive-Karoubi resolved envelope exists and has the stated universal property.
  • Invertible representation changes and noninvertible transformations can be separated formally through
    representation groupoids, bridge passports, and a typed directed category.
  • Information loss can be recorded separately as morphism collapse, object collapse, essential-image restriction,
    and loss of declared constructors or certificates.
  • A finite profiled atlas has a descent, theorem-transport, and invariant-assembly theorem once its hypotheses are
    verified.
  • Exact finite atlas models exist for graph holonomy, semilinear Galois descent, affine Mahler chart composition,
    split Mahler packet cores, and the normalized C-finite recurrence-rational-state triangle.
  • Finite graph transport has a unique maximal path-flat core, while full frame trivialization is equivalent to
    identity fundamental holonomy.
  • A canonical obstruction object exists relative to declared correction data; coarse vanishing can erase a nonzero
    fine obstruction.
  • Under the stated classical hypotheses, Hilbert 90 gives effective vector descent and the Brauer class is the exact
    projective-to-linear lifting obstruction.
  • Restricted complete invariants exist in several strata. The persistent C-finite slice has a complete global-to-packet
    minimality transfer theorem.
  • The unit purely periodic atlas over F 𝑝 is a verified heterogeneous atlas across linear, digital, and functional-
    Mahler strata. Its objects and morphisms descend effectively, and its periodic resolved invariant package is
    complete on the declared normalized semantic category.
  • The unit rational-mask atlas over F 𝑝 is a verified heterogeneous atlas across reduced functional-Mahler masks,
    minimal Cartier modules, and minimal digital automata. Its exact collapse obstruction separates the ultimately
    periodic locus from the genuinely non-C-finite automatic locus, and its descended invariant package is complete
    on the declared semantic category.
  • For a fixed radix and field, every regular sequence has a canonical minimal section module, a strict shortlex
    coordinate record, and a word-Hankel rank certificate. These charts satisfy effective descent, and the pointed
    section module is complete on the declared regular category.
  • Over a finite field, regularity is equivalent to automaticity and the minimal automaton is the finite section orbit.
    Over characteristic-zero fields, finite regular rank can coexist with an infinite kernel orbit.
  • Every integer-valued fixed-radix sequence of finite rational section rank has a canonical finite free raw section
    lattice and a canonical finite free integer-valued saturation of the same rank.
  • The Smith invariants and Fitting ideals of the finite saturation quotient determine the ghost kernel, semantic
    image size, missing quotient, good moduli, and prime-power generator profile exactly.
  • Every prime-power coefficient reduction has a finite minimal semantic section automaton, and the compatible
    full module tower reconstructs the embedded 𝑝-adic raw lattice.
  • 𝑝-adic completion, profinite completion, ordinary integral lifting, and actual section realization are distinct
    effectivity levels with explicit obstruction quotients.

                                                          109
Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                    Komorebi P.


  • Every integral unit series has unique fixed Euler exponents and unique divisor ghosts, with exact Möbius
    integrality conditions and coefficient recurrence.
  • Every integral multiplicative arithmetic function is reconstructed exactly from prime-power local series, and
    local Euler exponents and ghosts form complete additive coordinates for Dirichlet convolution.
  • On the multiplicative exponent locus, the divisor–ghost transform gives an effective four-chart atlas and a
    complete prime-power descended invariant.
  • Continuous Z 𝑝 -valued functions, their canonical Mahler coefficients, complete clopen branch packets, and
    branchwise Mahler coefficients form an effective normalized local atlas.
  • Affine Mahler kernels have finite triangular support, compose by exact mixed-radix substitution, and satisfy
    strict Chinese-remainder coherence.
  • Finite-degree affine channels have distinct characteristic-zero, integral, modular, and collective-packet bridge
    passports, with sharp minimal packet sizes under the printed hypotheses.
  • Declared monotone Mahler valuation profiles survive affine polyphase refinement, and principal-unit state
    packets acquire a certified linearly improving precision slope under 𝑝-power refinement.


15.2 Claims not established

The following statements are not proved here:

  • every mathematical object has a finite Komorebi presentation;
  • all strata are equivalent categories;
  • every bridge is invertible;
  • one global canonical minimal presentation exists across arbitrary grammars;
  • one finite invariant classifies every stratum;
  • equivalence of arbitrary presentations is decidable;
  • the full eight-stratum heterogeneous atlas already satisfies effective descent;
  • every automatic sequence has a rational functional-Mahler mask, or functional Mahler equations and Newton-
    Mahler interpolation are unrestrictedly equivalent;
  • one base-independent or all-ring canonical regular normal form, one minimal semiring realization theorem, or
    one cross-radix invariant compatible with every base;
  • the Block 06 integral lattice theorem automatically extends to arbitrary coefficient rings, non-Noetherian
    modules, semirings, all radices at once, or finite-data recognition of rational regularity;
  • unique Euler coordinates imply finite structural complexity, a minimal product compiler, or a canonical
    factorization across arbitrary product grammars;
  • ordinary Cauchy convolution and Dirichlet convolution are globally isomorphic, or prime-power seeds classify
    arithmetic functions without the multiplicativity axiom;
  • the multiplicative Euler-exponent locus is closed under ordinary Euler-product multiplication;
  • functional-Mahler coefficient equations and Newton–Mahler 𝑝-adic interpolation are canonically or unrestrict-
    edly equivalent;



                                                        110
Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                      Komorebi P.


  • every continuous 𝑝-adic function is locally analytic, every affine-kernel valuation lower bound is always sharp,
    or one optimal mixed-radix refinement tree has been constructed;
  • the local atlas already carries a complete combined spectral–local invariant or a universal state-recognition
    theorem;
  • algebraic and D-finite presentations are equivalent without retained branch data;
  • internal manuscript proof status implies independent peer review.

  Final status of the present paper. The project now has an explicit typed and packet-resolved common
  foundation, a proved additive-Karoubi envelope, profiled stratum and bridge passports, a directed information-
  loss calculus, a typed comparison of four obstruction theories, a proved finite assembly theorem, two exact
  affine-Mahler mechanisms, one fully effective normalized C-finite presentation atlas, and two fully effective
  heterogeneous digital/Mahler atlases. Block 05 adds the full fixed-radix field-valued regular atlas, a canonical
  word-Hankel and shortlex normal form, and the automatic finite-orbit theorem. Block 06 adds the canonical
  integral raw/saturated lattice pair, the exact Smith–Fitting reduction defect, finite prime-power semantic
  automata, and the 𝑝-adic/profinite effectivity hierarchy. Block 07 adds canonical fixed-basis Euler and ghost
  coordinates, prime-power effective descent for multiplicative functions, and the complete restricted divisor–
  ghost atlas. Block 08 adds the normalized continuous Newton–Mahler atlas, strict complete polyphase descent,
  sharp finite-degree channel packets, valuation-profile transport, and principal-unit precision amplification.
  The broader eight-stratum claim remains conditional on cross-radix and semiring compatibility, holonomic,
  algebraic, and nonlinear normalization, stronger local valuation and optimal mixed-radix theory, unrestricted
  product grammars, and effective invariant comparison beyond the normalized domains proved here.



16 Conclusion

The mathematically accurate form of Komorebi universality is stratified and relative. A resolved syntax does
not erase domain distinctions. It makes those distinctions explicit as preservation profiles, overlap categories,
directional information loss, and obstruction objects. The global object is not a single formula. It is a descent
category assembled from compatible local realizations.
The theorem concordance shows that most of the local machinery already exists. The finite additive-Karoubi
envelope gives an explicit common linear core. Blocks 01 and 02 add the data structure needed to use that core
honestly: stratum passports, normalized charts, typed overlaps, bridge-law certificates, information-loss profiles,
reversible cores, and obstruction-specific vanishing criteria. The C-finite three-chart theorem first shows that
presentation descent can be effective inside one stratum.
Block 03 crosses the first heterogeneous boundary. Minimal recurrences, reduced rational series, minimal digit
automata, and rational functional-Mahler masks form one coherent four-chart atlas on the unit purely periodic
domain. Block 04 then removes periodicity from the digital-functional-Mahler overlap. The Frobenius radical
identity identifies the semantic domain, the divisor-residue obstruction locates the rational collapse locus, and
the fixed-denominator compiler is reduced exactly to its canonical Cartier and 𝑝-kernel cores. Reduced masks,
minimal Cartier modules, and minimal DFAOs glue effectively for every unit rational-mask series, including
automatic sequences that are not C-finite.
Block 05 removes the scalar-mask assumption from the fixed-radix regular problem. The section orbit, its linear
span, the minimal digit realization, the strict shortlex basis, and the word-Hankel row space are now one normalized
object viewed through different charts. The finite-field locus recovers minimal automata, while characteristic-zero
examples separate finite rank from finite orbit.
Block 06 then resolves the first integral extension. The raw section lattice and the maximal integer-valued saturation


                                                        111
Stratified Universality for Resolved Representations                                                        Komorebi P.


are canonical finite free lattices inside the same rational section space. Their finite Smith–Fitting quotient gives the
exact modular defect. Prime-power semantic automata are finite and compatible, but completion introduces a new
hierarchy: a compatible tower may be 𝑝-adically effective without being integrally effective, and an integral lift
may still fail to be an actual section. The digit-sum example makes both strict containments explicit.
Block 07 resolves the first product–multiplicative extension. The fixed Euler basis gives a literal normal form
for every integral unit series, and the divisor ghost gives the exact logarithmic-derivative recurrence coordinate.
Multiplicative arithmetic functions descend from their prime-power local series, where local Euler exponents and
local ghosts linearize Dirichlet convolution. The divisor–ghost transform then identifies one restricted cross-stratum
overlap with effective descent and a complete prime-power invariant. The two global index monoids remain distinct:
the result is a typed essential-image bridge, not a universal equivalence of Cauchy and Dirichlet convolution.
Block 08 resolves the first continuous local extension. Mahler coefficients give canonical coordinates on 𝐶 (Z 𝑝 , Z 𝑝 ),
complete residue-coset packets give effective local descent, and affine coefficient kernels compose strictly under
mixed-radix refinement. Restriction to finite degree exposes the arithmetic change of passport from characteristic-
zero equivalence to integral nonsplitting, modular rank loss, and sharp collective packet recovery. Principal-unit
state dynamics enter the local atlas directionally with an explicit precision slope. The functional-Mahler and
Newton–Mahler languages remain separate unless a lifting and interpolation bridge is declared.
The remaining challenge is no longer fixed-base field, integral-lattice, fixed-Euler-basis, or continuous Newton–
Mahler normalization. It is to compare normalized objects across radices, extend them to coefficient rings and
semirings, prove effective finite-data recognition where possible, sharpen local kernel valuations and mixed-radix
optimization, and connect the verified charts to unrestricted product grammars, holonomic, algebraic, and nonlinear
strata while preserving exact bridge passports, explicit information loss, higher coherence, and classification targets
no broader than their proofs.

R Validation record

The validation summarized below concerns internal package integrity and exact finite verification. It is not an
external authenticity or peer-review certificate. The Block 03 verifier records 39,805 exact checks on 583 unit
primitive periodic words. The Block 04 verifier records 11,391 exact checks on 125 reduced rational masks over
three prime fields. The Block 05 fixed-radix verifier records 89,055 checks on bounded finite automata and exact
regular families. The Block 06 integral verifier records 697,958 exact Smith, modular, Fitting, prime-power,
digit-equivariant, and orbit-completion checks. The Block 07 product and multiplicative verifier records 595,964
exact Euler-coordinate, prime-power, Dirichlet-convolution, divisor–ghost, and named-family checks. The Block
08 local verifier records 229,743 exact affine-kernel, mixed-radix, packet-rank, decoder, valuation, and precision-
amplification checks. The combined Block 03–08 record is 1,663,916 exact checks with no failures. Detailed
tables and machine-readable JSON records are included in the source package.
